\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r_{*}}^{(t)}},{-y\mathbf{v}}\rangle
).
Thus we prove Inequalities (
B.20
) and (
B.21
) for
t=\underaccent{\bar}{T}_{1}
, and this can be further extended to the entire
[\bar{T}_{1},\bar{T}_{2}]
by another monotonicity argument since
\underaccent{\bar}{T}_{1}
is the local minima.

2.4

The remaining to is upper bound
\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r^{*}}^{(t)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle
for
t\in(\underaccent{\bar}{T}_{1},\bar{T}_{2})
. This is again a consequence of Lemma
B.5
, as exactly what has been done to upper bound
\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r^{*}}^{(t)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle,t\leq\bar{T}_{1}
in Step 1.

Now with the roadmap in mind, we are ready to dive into the details of every step.

Step 1: Pre-
\bar{T}_{1}
Analysis.

Lemma
A.4

indicates that the lower bound in Inequality (
B.20
) holds at initialization
t=0
under Assumption
B.1
.
Moreover, the upper bounds on the maximal initial inner products in Lemma
A.4
with Assumption
B.1
indicate that

\displaystyle|yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(0)})|

\displaystyle\leq 2\cdot\max_{r\in[m]}\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(0)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle^{2}\vee\max_{r\in[m]}\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(0)}},{-y\mathbf{u}}\rangle^{2}

(B.48)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\big(\sigma_{0}^{2}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}\big)\ll 1.

(B.49)

From this we know that
\bar{T}_{1}\geq 1
and the upper bound in Inequality (
B.19
) is true at
t=0
.

Then the first step to do is to extend the lower on
\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(t)}},{-y\mathbf{u}}\rangle
to
[1,\bar{T}_{1}]
. Definition of
\bar{T}_{1}
implies that
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\leq 1
for
t\in[0,\bar{T}_{1})
.
Therefore for
r\in\mathcal{U}_{-y,-}^{(0)}
, Equation (
B.3
) gives that

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(t+1)}},{-y\mathbf{u}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(t)}},{-y\mathbf{u}}\rangle+\frac{\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\big)\cdot\sigma^{\prime}\big(-\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(t)}},{-y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\big)

(B.50)

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(t)}},{-y\mathbf{u}}\rangle-\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\big)\cdot\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(t)}},{-y\mathbf{u}}\rangle

(B.51)

\displaystyle\geq\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(t)}},{-y\mathbf{u}}\rangle.

(B.52)

And furthermore

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(\bar{T}_{1})}},{-y\mathbf{u}}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(0)}},{-y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\geq-{\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}}.

(B.53)

Taking minimum with respect to
r\in[m]
gives the result. Same argument can be applied to
\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(\bar{T}_{1})}},{-y\mathbf{v}}\rangle
. So the lower bounds in Inequalities (
B.20
) and (
B.21
) hold for
t\in[1,\bar{T}_{1}]
.

Also, (
B.2
) and (
B.4
) imply that
\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle,r\in\mathcal{U}^{(0)}_{y,+}
and
\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)}},{y\mathbf{v}}\rangle,r\in\mathcal{V}^{(0)}_{y,+}
increase for all
t<\bar{T}_{1}
.
A natural consequence is that
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})
is non-decreasing in
t
in this stage, since every summand (possibly with the negative sign before) in the summation is non-decreasing.

Now we can prove the sign stability.
For
r\in\mathcal{U}_{y,+}^{(0)}
, we know from (
B.2
) that
\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle>\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(0)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle>0
, hence
r\in\mathcal{U}_{y,+}^{(t)}
is non-vanishing in
t
by induction.
Additionally, for
r\in\mathcal{U}_{y,-}^{(0)}
,
\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle
stays fixed in
t
, as mentioned in Appendix
B.1
.
Two points together ensure that

\mathcal{U}_{y,+}^{(t)}=\mathcal{U}_{y,+}^{(0)}
for
t\in[1,\bar{T}_{1}]
.

For
r\in\mathcal{U}_{-y,-}^{(0)}
, let’s take a closer look at Equation (
B.51
) with
t=0
:

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(1)}},{-y\mathbf{u}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(0)}},{-y\mathbf{u}}\rangle-\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(0)})\big)\cdot\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(0)}},{-y\mathbf{u}}\rangle

(B.54)

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(0)}},{-y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\cdot\underbrace{\left(1-\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(0)})\big)\right)}_{>0\text{ if }\eta<(1-o(1))/2\cdot m\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{-2}}.

(B.55)

Assumption
B.1
ensures that
\eta<0.4m\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{-2}<(1-o(1))/2\cdot m\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{-2}
so the sign change does not happen at
t=0
. As mentioned before,
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\leq 1
is non-decreasing in
t
at this stage, and putting them together we know that

\displaystyle 1-\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\big)\geq 0,\quad\forall t\in[1,\bar{T}_{1}).

(B.56)

The same sign stability can be verified for
\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(t)}},{-y\mathbf{v}}\rangle
, and therefore, the sign stability for
\mathcal{U}_{-y,-}^{(t)}
and
\mathcal{V}_{-y,-}^{(t)}
is ensured for
t\in[0,\bar{T}_{1}]
and the lower bound in Inequality (
B.20
) holds for
t\in[1,\bar{T}_{1}]
.

Now we turn to prove the upper bound (
B.19
). Note that
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\leq 1
for
t<\bar{T}_{1}
, and the definition of
T_{(\mathbf{v})}
then implies that

\displaystyle g(\mathbf{x},y;\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\leq 1+2\big({\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}}\big)^{2}\leq 1.05.

(B.57)

Now that the sign stability holds for
t\in[0,\bar{T}_{1})
, Lemma
B.5
gives that

\displaystyle\max_{r\in[m]}\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\leq\big(1.05\beta_{\mathbf{u}}^{*}m\big)^{1/2}.

(B.58)

Here
\beta^{*}_{\mathbf{u}}
is defined in
B.5
with the superscript
(0)
dropped.

Step 2.1: Bounding
\max_{r}\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle
for
t\in[\bar{T}_{1},\underaccent{\bar}{T}_{1})
.

In order for the upper bound to be tight, we need a lower bound on
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{1}-1)})
.
Let
\tilde{\eta}=2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}/m>1/2
, we have the following result.

Proposition B.9
.

For every
k\geq 1
, suppose that

\displaystyle E_{\bar{T}_{k}-1}\coloneqq\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(-\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(\bar{T}_{k}-1)}},{-y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\big)+\sigma\big(-\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(\bar{T}_{k}-1)}},{-y\mathbf{v}}\rangle\big)<\frac{\delta}{2}.

(B.59)

Then we have that

\displaystyle yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{k}-1)})\geq\frac{2+\tilde{\eta}-\sqrt{\tilde{\eta}^{2}+4\tilde{\eta}}}{2\tilde{\eta}}.

(B.60)

Moreover, it holds that

\displaystyle yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{k})})\leq yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{k}-1)})\cdot\left(1+\tilde{\eta}\cdot\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{k}-1)})\big)\right)^{2}+2E_{\bar{T}_{k}-1}.

(B.61)

Also, it is notable that the result here still holds for
\bar{T}_{k}\geq T_{(\mathbf{v})}
.

Proof of Proposition
B.9
.

See Appendix
B.6.2
for a detailed proof.
∎

Clearly, the conditions required for Proposition
B.9
is true for before
\bar{T}_{1}
.
So consider a one-step gradient descent at
t=\bar{T}_{1}-1
, by Proposition
B.9
and Equation (
B.2
),

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r^{*}}^{(\bar{T}_{1})}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r^{*}}^{(\bar{T}_{1}-1)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle+\frac{\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{1}-1)})\big)\cdot\sigma^{\prime}\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r^{*}}^{(\bar{T}_{1}-1)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\big)

(B.62)

\displaystyle\leq\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r^{*}}^{(\bar{T}_{1}-1)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\cdot\left(1+\tilde{\eta}\left(1-\frac{\tilde{\eta}+2-\sqrt{\tilde{\eta}^{2}+4\tilde{\eta}}}{2\tilde{\eta}}\right)\right)

(B.63)

\displaystyle\leq 1.5\cdot\big(1.05\beta_{\mathbf{u}}^{*}m\big)^{1/2}.

(B.64)

Here the first inequality above comes from Inequality (
B.60
), and the second inequality is derived from taking the suprema
\tilde{\eta}=1/2
and Inequality (
B.58
).
Additionally, we can further deliver an upper bound on
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{1})})
.
Inequality (
D.24
) and Inequality (
D.25
) that have been proved to be true on
[0,\bar{T}_{1}]
indicate that
E_{\bar{T}_{1}}=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\sigma_{0}^{2}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2})\ll 1
.
Combining with Inequality (
B.61
), it holds that

\displaystyle yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{1})})

\displaystyle\leq yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{k}-1)})\cdot\left(1+\tilde{\eta}\cdot\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{k}-1)})\big)\right)^{2}+o(1)

(B.65)

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\tilde{\eta}\cdot\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{k}-1)})\big)\right)^{2}+o(1)

(B.66)

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\big(\tilde{\eta}-1-\tilde{\eta}/2+\sqrt{\tilde{\eta}^{2}/4+\tilde{\eta}}\big)\right)^{2}+o(1)

(B.67)

\displaystyle=\bigl(\tilde{\eta}/2+\sqrt{\tilde{\eta}^{2}/4+\tilde{\eta}}\bigr)^{2}+o(1).

(B.68)

Since
\tilde{\eta}<1
, we know that
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{1})})\leq 3
and
|yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{1})})-1|\leq 2
.
(We keep
\tilde{\eta}
in the upper bound above for deriving a sufficient condition on
\tilde{\eta}
for the sign stability later.)

Now we look to the lower bound.
The definition of
\bar{T}_{1}
,
T_{(\mathbf{v})}
and Assumption
B.2
implies that
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})>1+\delta
, hence
g(\mathbf{x},y;\mathbf{W}^{(t)})>1+\delta-\delta=1
for
t\in[\bar{T}_{1},\underaccent{\bar}{T}_{1})
.
Thus Lemma
B.5
implies that

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r^{*}}^{(t)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle>(\beta^{*}_{\mathbf{u}}m)^{1/2},\quad t\in[\bar{T}_{1},\underaccent{\bar}{T}_{1}).

(B.69)

Step 2.2: Lower bounding
\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(\underaccent{\bar}{T}_{1})}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle
.

Note that
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\leq yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{1})})
from the local monotonicity. Consider doing one-step gradient descent with Equation (
B.2
):

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r^{*}}^{(\underaccent{\bar}{T}_{1})}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r^{*}}^{(\underaccent{\bar}{T}_{1}-1)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\cdot\left(1-\tilde{\eta}\cdot\big(yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\underaccent{\bar}{T}_{1}-1)})-1\big)\right)

(B.70)

\displaystyle\geq\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r^{*}}^{(\underaccent{\bar}{T}_{1}-1)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\cdot\left(1-\tilde{\eta}\cdot\big(yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{1}-1)})-1\big)\right)

(B.71)

\displaystyle\geq(\beta^{*}_{\mathbf{u}}m)^{1/2}\cdot\underbrace{\left(1-\tilde{\eta}\Big(\big(\tilde{\eta}/2+\sqrt{\tilde{\eta}^{2}/4+\tilde{\eta}}\big)^{2}-1\Big)-o(1)\right)}_{>0\text{ with }\tilde{\eta}<4/5}.

(B.72)

Here the last inequality is a consequence of Inequality (
B.68
) and the deterministic estimation that

\displaystyle\min_{\tilde{\eta}\in[1/2,4/5]}\Big\{1-\tilde{\eta}\Big(\big(\tilde{\eta}/2+\sqrt{\tilde{\eta}^{2}/4+\tilde{\eta}}\big)^{2}-1\Big)\Big\}>1/4.

(B.73)

We note that every step above is free of the lower bound in Inequality (
B.20
). Therefore, the sign stability is true on
[\bar{T}_{1},\underaccent{\bar}{T}_{1}]
, because all the inner products are non-decreasing in
t\in[\underaccent{\bar}{T}_{1},\bar{T}_{2}]

Step 2.3: Lower bounding

\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(\underaccent{\bar}{T}_{1})}},{-y\mathbf{u}}\rangle
and
\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(\underaccent{\bar}{T}_{1})}},{-y\mathbf{v}}\rangle
.

The key is to notice that the sign stability on
[0,\underaccent{\bar}{T}_{1}]
guarantees that for every
t\leq\underaccent{\bar}{T}_{1}-1
,

\displaystyle 1\pm\tilde{\eta}\cdot\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\big)>0,\quad 1\pm\tilde{\eta}\cdot\frac{\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}}{\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}\cdot\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\big)>0.

(B.74)

From Equation (
B.3
), we have that

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(\underaccent{\bar}{T}_{1})}},{-y\mathbf{u}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,}^{(0)}},{-y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\cdot\prod_{t=0}^{\underaccent{\bar}{T}_{1}-1}\left(1-\tilde{\eta}\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\big)\right)

(B.75)

\displaystyle\geq\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,}^{(0)}},{-y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\cdot\exp\left\{-\tilde{\eta}\sum_{t=0}^{\underaccent{\bar}{T}_{1}-1}\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\big)\right\}.

(B.76)

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\frac{\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r^{*}}^{(\underaccent{\bar}{T}_{1})}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle}{\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r^{*}}^{(0)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle}

\displaystyle=\prod_{t=0}^{\underaccent{\bar}{T}_{1}-1}\left(1+\tilde{\eta}\cdot\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\big)\right)\leq\exp\left\{\sum_{t=0}^{\underaccent{\bar}{T}_{1}-1}\tilde{\eta}\cdot\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\big)\right\}.

(B.77)

However,
\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r^{*}}^{(0)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\leq(\beta^{*}_{\mathbf{u}}m)^{1/2}\cdot{\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}}
and Inequality (
B.72
) imply that

\displaystyle\frac{\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r^{*}}^{(\underaccent{\bar}{T}_{1})}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle}{\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r^{*}}^{(0)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle}\geq\frac{(\beta^{*}_{\mathbf{u}}m)^{1/2}\cdot(1/4-o(1))}{(\beta^{*}_{\mathbf{u}}m)^{1/2}\cdot{\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}}}\geq 1.

(B.78)

Combining Inequalities (
B.77
) and (
B.78
), we can obtain that
\sum_{t=0}^{\underaccent{\bar}{T}_{1}-1}\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\big)\geq 0
. Together with Inequality (
B.76
), this in turns leads to

\displaystyle 0>\min_{r\in[m]}\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(\underaccent{\bar}{T}_{1})}},{-y\mathbf{u}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\min_{r\in[m]}\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(0)}},{-y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\cdot\exp\left\{-\tilde{\eta}\sum_{t=0}^{\underaccent{\bar}{T}_{1}-1}\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\big)\right\}

(B.79)

\displaystyle\geq\min_{r\in[m]}\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(0)}},{-y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\geq-{\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}}.

(B.80)

Analogously we have that

\displaystyle 0>\min_{r\in[m]}\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(\underaccent{\bar}{T}_{1})}},{-y\mathbf{v}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(0)}},{-y\mathbf{v}}\rangle\cdot\exp\left\{-\tilde{\eta}\cdot\frac{\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}}{\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}\cdot\sum_{t=0}^{\underaccent{\bar}{T}_{1}-1}\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\big)\right\}.

(B.81)

\displaystyle\geq\min_{r\in[m]}\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(0)}},{-y\mathbf{v}}\rangle\geq-{\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}}.

(B.82)

In conclusion, we have that

\displaystyle\max_{r\in[m]}\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\geq 0.2(\beta^{*}_{\mathbf{u}}m)^{1/2},\quad

\displaystyle t\in[\bar{T}_{1},\bar{T}_{2}],

(B.83)

\displaystyle\min_{r\in[m]}\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(t)}},{-y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\geq-{\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}},\quad

\displaystyle t\in[\bar{T}_{1},\bar{T}_{2}],

(B.84)

\displaystyle\min_{r\in[m]}\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(t)}},{-y\mathbf{v}}\rangle\geq-{\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}},\quad

\displaystyle t\in[\bar{T}_{1},\bar{T}_{2}].

(B.85)

Additionally, the lower bound on
\max_{r\in[m]}\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle
indicates that the sign stability holds on
[\bar{T}_{1},\bar{T}_{2})
, since the last drop step does not change the sign of
\langle{\mathbf{w}^{(t)}_{y,r}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle
.
Furthermore, once the
\mathbf{u}
-sign stability holds, the
\mathbf{v}
-sign stability can be easily derived.
To see this, we note that the
\mathbf{u}
-sign stability implies that, for any
t\in[0,T_{(\mathbf{v})}]
, it holds that
1\pm 2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)}))/m>0
. Now that
\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}>\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}
, one clearly sees that
1\pm 2\eta\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)}))/m>0
and the
\mathbf{v}
-sign stability holds.

Step 2.4: Upper bounding
\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle
for
t\in(\underaccent{\bar}{T}_{1},\bar{T}_{2})
.

This is exactly the same as the proof of Inequality (
B.58
) in
Step 1
, and is thus omitted.

Step 3. Finalizing proofs.

At this point, all the results in Lemma
B.6
have been proved to be true on
t\in[\bar{T}_{1},\bar{T}_{2})
. It is important to note that the only inductive hypothesis used for the local extension on
[\bar{T}_{1},\bar{T}_{2})
is the lower bound on
\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(t)}},{-y\mathbf{u}}\rangle
for
t\leq\bar{T}_{1}
.
The rest part merely comes from the definition of
\bar{T}_{1}
and
\underaccent{\bar}{T}_{2}
and Assumption
B.2
.
So repeating the same argument extends previous steps to all
t\leq T_{(\mathbf{v})}
.
∎

Remark B.10
.

In the proof, we implicitly utilize the fact that
\bar{T}_{k},\underaccent{\bar}{T}_{k}<+\infty,\;\forall k\geq 0
. One may conjecture that there could be cases that for some
k
,
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})>1
(resp.
<1
) for all
t\geq\bar{T}_{k}
(resp.
\underaccent{\bar}{T}_{k}
). However, Assumption
B.2
(guaranteed by tuning a proper
\eta
) indicates that this cannot happen. One can argue that once
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})>1
, the Assumption
B.2
enables the dynamic to bounce back towards
1
with at least exponential rate. Therefore,
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})
falls below
1+\delta
within a few steps and Assumption
B.2
forces
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})<1-\delta
, and
\underaccent{\bar}{T}_{k}<+\infty
. Same argument can be used to prove that
\bar{T}_{k}<+\infty
.

B.5
Proof of Fundamental Reasoning (Lemma
B.7
)

Proof of Lemma
B.7
.

Let
r^{*}:=\mathop{\mathrm{argmax}}_{r\in[m]}\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t_{0})}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle
. For the step
t_{0}<t_{1}
, (
B.2
) implies that

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r^{*}}^{(t_{1})}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r^{*}}^{(t_{0})}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle+\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\in[t_{0},t_{1}-1]:\\
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\geq 1\end{subarray}}\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)\cdot\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r^{*}}^{(s)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle

(B.86)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\in[t_{0},t_{1}-1]:\\
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})<1\end{subarray}}\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)\cdot\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r^{*}}^{(s)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle.

(B.87)

By Condition 4 in Lemma
B.7
, for all
s\in[t_{0},t_{1}]
,
\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(s)}},{y\mathbf{v}}\rangle)<\delta
.
Therefore by Assumption
B.2
, for
s
such that
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\geq 1
(and thus
>1+\delta
), it holds from (
B.10
) that

\displaystyle g(\mathbf{x},y;\mathbf{W}^{(t)})=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(s)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\big)\geq 1+\delta-\delta=1.

(B.88)

Hence by Lemma
B.5
, we have that

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r^{*}}^{(s)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\geq(\beta^{*}_{\mathbf{u}}m)^{1/2}.

(B.89)

On the other hand, by Conditions 3 in Lemma
B.7
, for all
s\in[t_{0},t_{1}]
, it holds that
\min_{r\in[m]}\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(s)}},{-y\mathbf{u}}\rangle>-0.1
and
\min_{r\in[m]}\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(s)}},{-y\mathbf{v}}\rangle>-0.1
, which lead to

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(-\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(s)}},{-y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\big)+\sigma\big(-\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(s)}},{-y\mathbf{v}}\rangle\big)\leq 2\times 0.1^{2}<0.05.

(B.90)

Therefore by Assumption
B.2
, for
s
such that
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\leq 1
(and thus
<1-\delta
), it holds from (
B.10
) that

\displaystyle g(\mathbf{x},y;\mathbf{W}^{(t)})=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(s)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\big)\leq 1-\delta+0.05.

(B.91)

Hence by Lemma
B.5
, we know that

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r^{*}}^{(s)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\leq(1.05-\delta)^{1/2}\cdot(\beta^{*}_{\mathbf{u}}m)^{1/2}.

(B.92)

Meanwhile, Condition 1 (
\mathbf{u}
-sign stability) and Condition 2 (boundedness) in Lemma
B.7
imply that

\displaystyle\left|\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r^{*}}^{(t_{1})}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle-\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r^{*}}^{(t_{0})}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\right|\leq\left|\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r^{*}}^{(t_{1})}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\right|\vee\left|\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r^{*}}^{(t_{0})}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\right|\leq B\cdot(\beta^{*}_{\mathbf{u}}m)^{1/2}.

(B.93)

Putting Inequality (
B.89
), (
B.92
), (
B.93
) and Equation (
B.87
) together, we have that

\displaystyle B\cdot(\beta^{*}_{\mathbf{u}}m)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\geq\left|\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\in[t_{0},t_{1}-1]:\\
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\geq 1\end{subarray}}\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)\cdot\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r^{*}}^{(s)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\right.

(B.94)

\displaystyle\qquad\left.+\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\in[t_{0},t_{1}-1]:\\
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})<1\end{subarray}}\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)\cdot\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r^{*}}^{(s)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\right|

(B.95)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\in[t_{0},t_{1}-1]:\\
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\geq 1\end{subarray}}\big(yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})-1\big)\cdot\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r^{*}}^{(s)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle

(B.96)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\in[t_{0},t_{1}-1]:\\
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})<1\end{subarray}}\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)\cdot\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r^{*}}^{(s)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle

(B.97)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot(\beta^{*}_{\mathbf{u}}m)^{1/2}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\in[t_{0},t_{1}-1]:\\
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\geq 1\end{subarray}}\big(yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})-1\big)

(B.98)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot(\beta^{*}_{\mathbf{u}}m)^{1/2}\cdot(1.05-\delta)^{1/2}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\in[t_{0},t_{1}-1]:\\
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})<1\end{subarray}}\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big).

(B.99)

This is also equivalent to

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\in[t_{0},t_{1}-1]:\\
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})<1\end{subarray}}\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)\cdot\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r^{*}}^{(s)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\in[t_{0},t_{1}-1]:\\
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\geq 1\end{subarray}}(1.05-\delta)^{-1/2}\cdot\big(yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})-1\big)

(B.100)

\displaystyle\qquad-\underbrace{\frac{mB}{2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}\sqrt{1.05-\delta}}}_{\coloneqq\Delta(B)}.

(B.101)

Now we are ready to lower bound the summation that we are interested in.
Since

\displaystyle|\{s\in[t_{0},t_{1}-1]:yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})<1\}|+|\{s\in[t_{0},t_{1}-1]:yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})>1\}|=t_{1}-t_{0},

(B.102)

we have either
|\{s\in[t_{0},t_{1}-1]:yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})>1\}|>(t_{1}-t_{0})/2
, which by (
B.101
) implies that

\displaystyle\sum_{s=t_{0}}^{t_{1}-1}\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\in[t_{0},t_{1}-1]:\\
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})<1\end{subarray}}\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\in[t_{0},t_{1}-1]:\\
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})>1\end{subarray}}\big(yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})-1\big)

(B.103)

\displaystyle\geq\big((1.05-\delta)^{-1/2}-1\big)\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\in[t_{0},t_{1}-1]:\\
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})>1\end{subarray}}\big(yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})-1\big)-\Delta(B)

(B.104)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\big(\delta(1.05-\delta)^{-1/2}-\delta\big)\cdot(t_{1}-t_{0})-\Delta(B),

(B.105)

or
|\{s\in[t_{0},t_{1}-1]:yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})<1\}|>(t-t_{0})/2
, which by (
B.101
) implies that

\displaystyle\sum_{s=t_{0}}^{t_{1}-1}\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\in[t_{0},t_{1}-1]:\\
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})<1\end{subarray}}\big(yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})-1\big)-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\in[t_{0},t_{1}-1]:\\
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})>1\end{subarray}}\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)

\displaystyle\geq\big(1-(1.05-\delta)^{1/2}\big)\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\in[t_{0},t_{1}-1]:\\
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})<1\end{subarray}}\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)-(1.05-\delta)^{1/2}\cdot\Delta(B)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\big(\delta-\delta(1.05-\delta)^{1/2}\big)\cdot(t_{1}-t_{0})-(1.05-\delta)^{1/2}\cdot\Delta(B).

(B.106)

In both two cases we have used Assumption
B.2
to bound
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})
from
1
.
Combining (
B.105
) and (
B.106
), we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{s=t_{0}}^{t_{1}-1}\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)\geq\frac{1}{2}\big(\delta-\delta(1.05-\delta)^{1/2}\big)\cdot(t_{1}-t_{0})-\Delta(B).

(B.107)

Now plugging in the definition of
\Delta(B)
in (
B.101
), we have proved the linear increasing lower bound for the summation, which is the first conclusion of Lemma
B.7
.

Then we turn to prove the second conclusion of Lemma
B.7
.
For simplicity, we denote
\alpha:=\|\mathbf{v}\|^{2}_{2}/\|\mathbf{u}\|^{2}_{2}
and
\epsilon=(\delta-\delta(1.05-\delta)^{1/2})/4
.
Note that from the
\mathbf{v}
-sign stability (Condition 1) and (
B.4
), we have that

\displaystyle 1+\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\big)>0,\quad\forall t\in[t_{0},t_{1}-1].

(B.108)

Therefore, we can lower bound the logarithmic ratio
\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)}},{y\mathbf{v}}\rangle/\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(0)}},{y\mathbf{v}}\rangle
for
r\in\mathcal{V}_{y,+}
as
(recall that
\tilde{\eta}=2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}/m
)

\displaystyle\sum_{t=t_{0}}^{t_{1}-1}\log\Big(1+\alpha\tilde{\eta}\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\big)\Big)

(B.109)

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{t=t_{0}}^{t_{1}-1}\int_{0}^{\alpha}\frac{\tilde{\eta}\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\big)}{1+\tilde{\eta}z\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\big)}dz

(B.110)

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{t=t_{0}}^{t_{1}-1}\int_{0}^{\alpha}\frac{\tilde{\eta}\cdot\Big(\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\big)+2\Big)}{1+\tilde{\eta}z\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\big)}dz-\sum_{t=t_{0}}^{t_{1}-1}\int_{0}^{\alpha}\frac{2\tilde{\eta}}{1+\tilde{\eta}z\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\big)}dz.

(B.111)

Note that by Condition 5 in Lemma
E.3
,
-2\leq 1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\leq 1
, which further lower bounds (
B.111
) as

(
B.111
)

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{t=t_{0}}^{t_{1}-1}\int_{0}^{\alpha}\frac{\tilde{\eta}\cdot\Big(\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\big)+2\Big)}{1+\tilde{\eta}z}dz-\sum_{t=t_{0}}^{t_{1}-1}\int_{0}^{\alpha}\frac{2\tilde{\eta}}{1-2\tilde{\eta}z}dz

(B.112)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\alpha}\frac{\tilde{\eta}\cdot\Big(\sum_{t=t_{0}}^{t_{1}-1}\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\big)+2(t_{1}-t_{0})\Big)}{1+\tilde{\eta}z}dz-\int_{0}^{\alpha}\frac{2\tilde{\eta}(t_{1}-t_{0})}{1-2\tilde{\eta}z}dz.

(B.113)

Now applying (
B.107
) to the summation in (
B.113
), we can arrive at

(
B.113
)

\displaystyle\geq\int_{0}^{\alpha}\frac{\tilde{\eta}\cdot\Big(2\epsilon(t_{1}-t_{0})-\Delta(B)+2(t_{1}-t_{0})\Big)}{1+\tilde{\eta}z}dz-\int_{0}^{\alpha}\frac{2\tilde{\eta}(t_{1}-t_{0})}{1-2\tilde{\eta}z}dz

(B.114)

\displaystyle\geq\Big(2\epsilon(t_{1}-t_{0})-\Delta(B)+2(t_{1}-t_{0})\Big)\cdot\log(1+\alpha\tilde{\eta})+(t_{1}-t_{0})\cdot\log(1-2\alpha\tilde{\eta})

(B.115)

\displaystyle\geq\big(2\epsilon(t_{1}-t_{0})-\Delta(B)\big)\cdot\log(1+\alpha\tilde{\eta})+(t_{1}-t_{0})\cdot\log\Big((1+\alpha\tilde{\eta})^{2}\cdot(1-2\alpha\tilde{\eta})\Big).

(B.116)

To further lower bound (
B.116
), we note that
\alpha\tilde{\eta}>\log(1+\alpha\tilde{\eta})\geq\frac{1}{2}\alpha\tilde{\eta}
since by Assumption
B.1
,
0<\alpha\tilde{\eta}<1
.
Furthermore, Assumption
B.1
guarantees that
\alpha<\epsilon/2=(\delta-\delta\cdot(1.05-\delta)^{1/2})/8
and
2\alpha^{3}\tilde{\eta}^{3}+3\alpha^{2}\tilde{\eta}^{2}\leq 4\alpha^{2}<1/5\wedge 2\epsilon/5
, so we have that

\displaystyle\log\Big((1+\alpha\tilde{\eta})^{2}\cdot(1-2\alpha\tilde{\eta})\Big)

\displaystyle=\log\big(1-3\alpha^{2}\tilde{\eta}^{2}-2\alpha^{3}\tilde{\eta}^{3}\big)

(B.117)

\displaystyle=-\log\left(1+\frac{3\alpha^{2}\tilde{\eta}^{2}+2\alpha^{3}\tilde{\eta}^{3}}{1-3\alpha^{2}\tilde{\eta}^{2}-2\alpha^{3}\tilde{\eta}^{3}}\right)

(B.118)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{-3\alpha^{2}\tilde{\eta}^{2}-2\alpha^{3}\tilde{\eta}^{3}}{1-3\alpha^{2}\tilde{\eta}^{2}-2\alpha^{3}\tilde{\eta}^{3}}

(B.119)

\displaystyle\geq-5\alpha^{2}.

(B.120)

Putting (
B.116
) and (
B.120
) together, we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{t=t_{0}}^{t_{1}-1}\log\Big(1+\alpha\tilde{\eta}\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\big(\alpha\tilde{\eta}\epsilon-5\alpha^{2}\big)\cdot(t_{1}-t_{0})-\Delta(B)\cdot\log(1+\alpha\tilde{\eta})

(B.121)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\alpha\tilde{\eta}\epsilon\cdot(t_{1}-t_{0})-\Delta(B)\alpha\tilde{\eta}

(B.122)

And consequently we have the following result, for all
r\in\mathcal{V}_{y,+}
,

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t_{1})}},{y\mathbf{v}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t_{0})}},{y\mathbf{v}}\rangle\cdot\prod_{t=t_{0}}^{t_{1}-1}\Big(1+\alpha\tilde{\eta}\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\big)\Big)

(B.123)

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t_{0})}},{y\mathbf{v}}\rangle\cdot\exp\left\{\sum_{t=t_{0}}^{t_{1}-1}\log\Big(1+\alpha\tilde{\eta}\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\big)\Big)\right\}

(B.124)

\displaystyle\geq\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t_{0})}},{y\mathbf{v}}\rangle\cdot\exp\left\{\frac{1}{2}\alpha\tilde{\eta}\epsilon\cdot(t_{1}-t_{0})-\Delta(B)\alpha\tilde{\eta}\right\},

(B.125)

where in the last inequality we use (
B.122
).
Plugging in the expression of
\epsilon
,
\alpha
,
\tilde{\eta}
, and
\Delta(B)
, we have proved the second conclusion of Lemma
B.7
.
This concludes the proof of Lemma
B.7
.
∎

B.6
Proof of Technical Results

B.6.1
Proof of Proposition
B.8

Proof of Proposition
B.8
.

We want to track the sequence (
B.32
) after it falls below
\delta
. To this end, we define two stopping times

\displaystyle T_{(\mathbf{v}),\delta,L}

\displaystyle=\min\left\{t\geq T_{(\mathbf{v})}:\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)}},{y\mathbf{v}}\rangle\big)<\delta\right\},

(B.126)

\displaystyle T_{(\mathbf{v})}^{+,2}

\displaystyle=\min\left\{t\geq T_{(\mathbf{v}),\delta,L}:\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)}},{y\mathbf{v}}\rangle\big)\geq\delta\right\}.

(B.127)

If
T_{(\mathbf{v}),\delta,L}=+\infty
, then the proof is over.
Otherwise we prove that, before
T_{(\mathbf{v})}^{+,2}\leq+\infty
(possibly equal), the sequence never falls below
\delta/2
.

Let’s take a closer look at the controls over the negative parts while the weak signal remain learned. Note that for
t\in[T_{(\mathbf{v})},T_{(\mathbf{v}),\delta,L}]
and
r\in\mathcal{V}_{y,+}
, we have that

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)}},{y\mathbf{v}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(0)}},{y\mathbf{v}}\rangle\cdot\prod_{s=0}^{t-1}\left(1+\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{v}\|}{m}\cdot\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)\right)

(B.128)

\displaystyle\leq\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(0)}},{y\mathbf{v}}\rangle\cdot\exp\left\{\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)\right\}.

(B.129)

Meanwhile, for
t\in[T_{(\mathbf{v})},T_{(\mathbf{v}),\delta,L}]
, we have that
\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)}},{y\mathbf{v}}\rangle>(\beta_{\mathbf{v}}^{*}m\delta)^{1/2}\gg\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(0)}},{y\mathbf{v}}\rangle
. Hence

\displaystyle\exp\left\{\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)\right\}>1,\quad\forall t\in[T_{(\mathbf{v})},T_{(\mathbf{v}),\delta,L}-1],

(B.130)

and in consequence we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)>0,\quad\forall t\in[T_{(\mathbf{v})},T_{(\mathbf{v}),\delta,L}-1].

(B.131)

On the other hand, for
r\in\mathcal{V}_{-y,-}
, it holds that
\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(0)}},{-y\mathbf{v}}\rangle<0
and thus by (
B.131
) we have that

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(t)}},{-y\mathbf{v}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(0)}},{-y\mathbf{v}}\rangle\cdot\prod_{s=0}^{t-1}\left(1-\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)\right)

(B.132)

\displaystyle\geq\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(0)}},{-y\mathbf{v}}\rangle\cdot\exp\left\{-\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)\right\}

(B.133)

\displaystyle\geq\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(0)}},{-y\mathbf{v}}\rangle\geq-{\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}},

(B.134)

for all
t\in[T_{(\mathbf{v})},T_{(\mathbf{v}),\delta,L}]
. Analogously, we also have that

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(t)}},{-y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\geq\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(0)}},{-y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\geq-{\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}}

(B.135)

for all
t\in[T_{(\mathbf{v})},T_{(\mathbf{v}),\delta,L}]
. Therefore, we have that for all
t\in[T_{(\mathbf{v})},T_{(\mathbf{v}),\delta,L}]
,

\displaystyle E_{t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(-\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(t)}},{-y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\big)+\sigma\big(-\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(t)}},{-y\mathbf{v}}\rangle\big)

(B.136)

\displaystyle\leq 2\cdot\max_{r\in[m]}\left\{\sigma\big(-\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(t)}},{-y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\big)\vee\sigma\big(-\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(t)}},{-y\mathbf{v}}\rangle\big)\right\}

(B.137)

\displaystyle\leq 2\big({\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}}\big)^{2}

(B.138)

\displaystyle\ll\frac{\delta}{2}.

(B.139)

This allows us to leverage Proposition
B.9
to upper bound
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})
for
t\in[T_{(\mathbf{v})},T_{(\mathbf{v}),\delta,L}]
.
Specifically,
we locate the last step before
T_{(\mathbf{v}),\delta,L}
when
yf
just bounces up over
1
, which is,

\displaystyle\bar{T}_{k^{*}}\coloneqq\max\left\{\bar{T}_{k}:\bar{T}_{k}\leq T_{(\mathbf{v}),\delta,L}\right\},

(B.140)

where
\bar{T}_{k}
is defined in (
B.45
).
Then Proposition
B.9
with Inequality (
B.139
) implies that

\displaystyle yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{k^{*}})})

\displaystyle\leq yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{k^{*}}-1)})\cdot\Big(1+\tilde{\eta}\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{k}-1)})\big)\Big)^{2}+2E_{\bar{T}_{k^{*}}-1}

(B.141)

\displaystyle\leq\Big(1+\tilde{\eta}\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{k^{*}}-1)})\big)\Big)^{2}+o(1)

(B.142)

\displaystyle\leq\Big({1+\tilde{\eta}\Big(1-\frac{1}{2\tilde{\eta}}\big(\tilde{\eta}+2-\sqrt{\tilde{\eta}^{2}+4\tilde{\eta}}\big)\Big)}\Big)^{2}+o(1)

(B.143)

\displaystyle\leq 3,

(B.144)

where we have applied the fact that
\tilde{\eta}<1
.
On the other hand, we have that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(\bar{T}_{k^{*}}-1)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\big)\leq 1.05,

(B.145)

for which Lemma
B.5
indicates that
\max_{r\in[m]}\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(\bar{T}_{k^{*}}-1)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\leq(1.05\beta^{*}_{\mathbf{u}}m)^{1/2}
.
As in Inequality (
B.64
), one step gradient descent then gives that

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(\bar{T}_{k^{*}})}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\leq\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(\bar{T}_{k^{*}}-1)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\cdot\left(1+\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{k^{*}}-1)})\big)\right)\leq 1.5\cdot\big(1.05\beta^{*}_{\mathbf{u}}m\big)^{1/2}.

(B.146)

Now we consider the scale of these inner products right at
T_{(\mathbf{v}),\delta,L}
.
From the definitions of
T_{(\mathbf{v}),\delta,L}
and
\bar{T}_{k^{*}}
we know that
yf_{t}>1
between these steps (otherwise the sequence wouldn’t fall below
\delta
).
Thus by (
B.144
),

\displaystyle 1<yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})<yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{k^{*}})})\leq 3,\quad\forall t\in[\bar{T}_{k^{*}},T_{(\mathbf{v}),\delta,L}].

(B.147)

We first state that
\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(T_{(\mathbf{v}),\delta,L})}},{y\mathbf{v}}\rangle)
is not far away from
\delta
.
Note that by (
B.131
),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(T_{(\mathbf{v}),\delta,L})}},{y\mathbf{v}}\rangle\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(T_{(\mathbf{v}),\delta,L}-1)}},{y\mathbf{v}}\rangle\big)\cdot\left(1+\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(T_{(\mathbf{v}),\delta,L}-1)})\big)\right)

(B.148)

\displaystyle\geq\delta\cdot\left(1+\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot(1-3)\right)

(B.149)

\displaystyle\geq\delta\cdot\left(1-2\cdot\frac{\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}}{\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}\right)

(B.150)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{3}{4}\delta.

(B.151)

Last inequality uses the fact that
\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}/\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 1/4
.
This shows that the sequence does not fall below
3\delta/4
at the step
T_{(\mathbf{v}),\delta,L}
.
In the sequel, we study the behavior of the sequence after step
T_{(\mathbf{v}),\delta,L}
.

Firstly, by Inequalities (
B.134
) and (
B.135
) with
t=T_{(\mathbf{v}),\delta,L}
, we obtain that the negative parts satisfy

\displaystyle\min_{r\in[m]}\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(T_{(\mathbf{v}),\delta,L})}},{-y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\geq-{\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}},

(B.152)

\displaystyle\min_{r\in[m]}\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(T_{(\mathbf{v}),\delta,L})}},{-y\mathbf{v}}\rangle\geq-{\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}}.

(B.153)

which by definition of
E_{t}
in (
B.139
) implies that

\displaystyle E_{T_{(\mathbf{v}),\delta,L}}\leq 2\left({\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}}\right)^{2}\ll 1.

(B.154)

For the positive part, we have that

\displaystyle\max_{r\in[m]}\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(T_{(\mathbf{v}),\delta,L})}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle<\max_{r\in[m]}\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(\bar{T}_{k^{*}})}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle<1.5\cdot(1.05\beta^{*}_{\mathbf{u}}m)^{1/2},

(B.155)

and by the same argument as Inequality (
B.72
) we can obtain the lower bound of
\max_{r\in[m]}\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(T_{(\mathbf{v}),\delta,L})}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle
,

\displaystyle\max_{r\in[m]}\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(T_{(\mathbf{v}),\delta,L})}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\max_{r\in[m]}\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(T_{(\mathbf{v}),\delta,L}-1)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\left(1+\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(T_{(\mathbf{v}),\delta,L}-1)})\big)\right)

(B.156)

\displaystyle\geq 0.2\cdot\big(\beta^{*}_{\mathbf{u}}m\big)^{1/2},

(B.157)

and finally for
t\in[T_{(\mathbf{v}),\delta,L},T_{(\mathbf{v})}^{+,2}]
, we have that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r}\sigma(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)}},{y\mathbf{v}}\rangle)<\delta.

(B.158)

With all these initial conditions (
B.154
), (
B.155
), (
B.157
), and (
B.158
), one can then consider all the steps
\underaccent{\bar}{T}_{k^{\prime}}
,
\bar{T}_{k^{\prime}}>T_{(\mathbf{v}),\delta,L}
and apply an inductive argument exactly the same as in the proof of Lemma
B.6
to conclude that for all
t\in[T_{(\mathbf{v}),\delta,L},T_{(\mathbf{v})}^{+,2}]
it holds that

\displaystyle 0.2\cdot(\beta^{*}_{\mathbf{u}}m)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{r}\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\leq 1.5\cdot(1.05\beta^{*}_{\mathbf{u}}m)^{1/2},

(B.159)

\displaystyle\min_{r}\langle{-\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(t)}},{-y\mathbf{u}}\rangle

\displaystyle>-{\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}},

(B.160)

\displaystyle\min_{r}\langle{-\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(t)}},{-y\mathbf{v}}\rangle

\displaystyle>-{\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}},

(B.161)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\leq 3,

(B.162)

and the sign stability is also true throughout
t\in[T_{(\mathbf{v}),\delta,L},T_{(\mathbf{v})}^{+,2}]
.
Therefore, Lemma
B.7
and (
B.151
) implies that for any
t\in[T_{(\mathbf{v}),\delta,L},T_{(\mathbf{v})}^{+,2}]
, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r}\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,v}^{(t)}},{y\mathbf{v}}\rangle\big)\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r}\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,v}^{(T_{(\mathbf{v}),\delta,L})}},{y\mathbf{v}}\rangle\big)\cdot\exp\left\{-2\cdot\frac{\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}}{\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}\cdot\frac{1.5\sqrt{1.05}}{\sqrt{1.05-\delta}}\right\}\geq\frac{1}{2}\delta.

(B.163)

In conclusion, we have obtained that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}},{y\mathbf{v}}\rangle\big)\geq\delta/2,\quad\forall t\in[T_{(\mathbf{v}),\delta,L},T_{(\mathbf{v})}^{+,2}].

(B.164)

If
T_{(\mathbf{v})}^{+,2}\neq+\infty
, repeat the above argument and we can finish the proof of Proposition
B.8
.
∎

B.6.2
Proof of Proposition
B.9

Proof of Proposition
B.9
.

We continue with the notation
\tilde{\eta}=2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}/m
. Define the function

\displaystyle h_{\tilde{\eta}}(z)\coloneqq\big(1+\tilde{\eta}(1-z)\big)^{2}\cdot z.

(B.165)

Note that
\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}
and that

\displaystyle\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{k}-1)})\big)<\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}<1,

(B.166)

from the definition of
\bar{T}_{k}-1
.
Thus we have the following,

\displaystyle yf(\mathbf{x},y;\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{k})})

(B.167)

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(\bar{T}_{k}-1)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\big)\cdot\left(1+\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{k}-1)})\big)\right)^{2}

(B.168)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(\bar{T}_{k}-1)}},{y\mathbf{v}}\rangle\big)\cdot\left(1+\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{k}-1)})\big)\right)^{2}

(B.169)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(-\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(\bar{T}_{k}-1)}},{-y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\big)\cdot\left(1-\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{k}-1)})\big)\right)^{2}

(B.170)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(-\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(\bar{T}_{k}-1)}},{-y\mathbf{v}}\rangle\big)\cdot\left(1-\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{k}-1)})\big)\right)^{2}

(B.171)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\Big(\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(\bar{T}_{k}-1)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\big)+\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(\bar{T}_{k}-1)}},{y\mathbf{v}}\rangle\big)\Big)\cdot\left(1+\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{k}-1)})\big)\right)^{2}

(B.172)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\Big(\sigma\big(-\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(\bar{T}_{k}-1)}},{-y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\big)+\sigma\big(-\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(\bar{T}_{k}-1)}},{-y\mathbf{v}}\rangle\big)\Big)

(B.173)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\left(1-\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{k}-1)})\big)\right)^{2}

(B.174)

\displaystyle\qquad=yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{k}-1)})\cdot\left(1+\tilde{\eta}\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{k}-1)})\big)\right)^{2}

(B.175)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\Big(\sigma\big(-\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(\bar{T}_{k}-1)}},{-y\mathbf{u}}\rangle\big)+\sigma\big(-\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-y,r}^{(\bar{T}_{k}-1)}},{-y\mathbf{v}}\rangle\big)\Big)\cdot\underbrace{\left(2\tilde{\eta}\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{k}-1)})\big)\right)^{2}}_{<2\tilde{\eta}<2}

(B.176)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{k}-1)})\cdot\left(1+\tilde{\eta}\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{k}-1)})\big)\right)^{2}+2E_{\bar{T}_{k}-1}.

(B.177)

Thus by (
B.177
) we have proved the second conclusion of Proposition
B.9
.
In the following, we prove the first conclusion of Proposition
B.9
.

By Assumption
B.2
, we know that
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{k})})>1+\delta
.
The definition of
T_{(\mathbf{v})}
along with with Inequality (
B.177
) imply that

\displaystyle h_{\tilde{\eta}}\big(f(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{k}-1)})\big)=yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{k}-1)})\cdot\left(1+\tilde{\eta}\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{k}-1)})\big)\right)^{2}\geq 1+\delta-\delta=1.

(B.178)

Now it suffices to consider the equation
h_{\tilde{\eta}}-1=0
, and one can easily verify that it has three roots

\displaystyle z_{1}

\displaystyle=1,

(B.179)

\displaystyle z_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\tilde{\eta}+2-\sqrt{\tilde{\eta}^{2}+4\tilde{\eta}}}{2\tilde{\eta}},

(B.180)

\displaystyle z_{3}

\displaystyle=\frac{\tilde{\eta}+2+\sqrt{\tilde{\eta}^{2}+4\tilde{\eta}}}{2\tilde{\eta}}>\frac{2\tilde{\eta}+2}{2\tilde{\eta}}>1.

(B.181)

And the second root
z_{2}<1
if and only if
\tilde{\eta}>1/2
.

Now if
0<\tilde{\eta}\leq 1/2
, then
z_{1}\leq z_{2}<z_{3}
, and then
h(z)<1
for
z<z_{1}=1
. Therefore Inequality (
B.178
) implies that
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{k}-1)})\geq z_{1}=1
, and recursively implies that
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(0)})\geq 1
, which contradicts with the fact that
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(0)})\leq 2{\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}}
.
So in order for
\bar{T}_{1}<+\infty
, we must have
\tilde{\eta}>1/2
. In conclusion, one necessary condition for the stable oscillation Assumption
B.2
is that
\tilde{\eta}>1/2
, and therefore

\displaystyle yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}-1)})\geq z_{2}=\frac{\tilde{\eta}+2-\sqrt{\tilde{\eta}^{2}+4\tilde{\eta}}}{2\tilde{\eta}}.

(B.182)

This finishes the proof of Proposition
B.9
.
∎

B.7
Discussion: Necessary Condition for
\delta
-Oscillation

Inequality (
B.68
) provides an upper bound involving
\tilde{\eta}
, which should be
compatible with Assumption
B.2
, hence

\displaystyle\big(\tilde{\eta}/2+\sqrt{\tilde{\eta}^{2}/4+\tilde{\eta}}\big)^{2}>1+\delta\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\tilde{\eta}>(1+\delta^{-1})\big(\sqrt{1+\delta}-1\big).

(B.183)

One can verify with software that RHS is monotonically increasing in
\delta\in[0,1]
with minimal value
0.5
, when
\tilde{\eta}=0
, which is in line with the weakest oscillation condition discovered in the last part. And the maximal value taken at
\delta=1
is less than
0.83
.

On the other hand, Inequality (
B.60
) should also be compatible with the Assumption
4.2
, which indicates

\displaystyle 1-\delta>yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(\bar{T}_{k-1}}))>\frac{2+\tilde{\eta}-\sqrt{\tilde{\eta}^{2}+4\tilde{\eta}}}{2\tilde{\eta}}.

(B.184)

The readers can see that it is equivalent to
\tilde{\eta}>\delta^{-1}((1-\delta)^{-1/2}-1)
. Furthermore, we have that
\delta^{-1}((1-\delta)^{-1/2}-1)>(1+\delta^{-1})(\sqrt{1+\delta}-1)
thus it is a stronger requirement on
\eta
.

Appendix C
Single Training Data Case: Small Learning Rate Regime

This section focuses on training our model with single noiseless data point
(\mathbf{x},y)
, where
\mathbf{x}=(y\mathbf{u},y\mathbf{v})
contains two signal patches with
\mathbf{u}
much stronger than
\mathbf{v}
. Therefore, the whole objective can be rearranged by

\displaystyle L(\mathbf{W})=\frac{1}{2}\big(f(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W})-y\big)^{2}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\sum_{j\in\{\pm 1\}}\frac{j}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r},y\mathbf{u}\rangle)+\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r},y\mathbf{v}\rangle)-y\right)^{2}.

(C.1)

In this simplified setting, each weight vector is updated by

\displaystyle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t+1)}=\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)}-\frac{jy\eta}{m}\cdot\big(f(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})-y\big)\cdot\left(\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y\mathbf{u}\rangle)\mathbf{u}+\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y\mathbf{v}\rangle)\mathbf{v}\right).

(C.2)

Then we can directly obtain the following updating rules of the inner products,

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t+1)},\mathbf{u}\rangle=\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}\rangle-\frac{jy\eta}{m}\cdot\big(f(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})-y\big)\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y\mathbf{u}\rangle)\cdot\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2},

(C.3)

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t+1)},\mathbf{v}\rangle=\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}\rangle-\frac{jy\eta}{m}\cdot\big(f(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})-y\big)\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y\mathbf{v}\rangle)\cdot\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2},

(C.4)

since
\mathbf{u}
and
\mathbf{v}
are assumed to be orthogonal in our data generation model.
In this section, we also denote the fitting residual at iteration
t
as
\ell^{(t)}=f(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})-y
for convenience.

To better prepare for the analysis of this section, we single out the following concentration results from Appendix
A
which provides a high-probability bound on the initialization
\mathbf{W}^{(0)}
.

Lemma C.1

(Initialization)
.

Suppose that
d=\Omega(\log(m/p))
and
m=\Omega(\log(1/p))
. Then with probability at least
1-p
, there holds

\displaystyle\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{u}\|/2\leq

\displaystyle\max_{r\in[m]}\langle\mathbf{w}^{(t)}_{j,r},j\mathbf{u}\rangle\leq\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{u}\|,

(C.5)

\displaystyle\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{v}\|/2\leq

\displaystyle\max_{r\in[m]}\langle\mathbf{w}^{(t)}_{j,r},j\mathbf{v}\rangle\leq\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{v}\|,

(C.6)

for all
j\in\{\pm 1\}
.

The result of this section relies on the following conditions on the data model and the initialization.

Assumption C.2

(Conditions on hyperparameters)
.

Suppose that the following holds:

1.

The weight initialization scale
\sigma_{0}=\widetilde{\Theta}(\|\mathbf{u}\|^{-1}_{2})
;

2.

The signal strength
\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}>\widetilde{\Omega}(m^{2})\cdot\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}
;

3.

The dimension
d
satisfies
d=\Omega(\mathrm{polylog}(m))
.

Theorem C.3

(Restatement of Proposition
3.1
)
.

Under Assumption
C.2
, choosing the learning rate
\eta\leq m/6\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}
small enough and
\epsilon=0.01
, then with probability at least
1-p=1-1/\mathrm{poly}(d)
, there exist

T^{\dagger}=\frac{m}{\eta(1-\tau)\|\mathbf{u}\|^{2}_{2}}\log\left(\frac{2\iota}{\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}}\right),\quad T=T^{\dagger}+\left\lfloor\frac{Cm^{3}}{2\eta\epsilon\|\mathbf{u}\|^{2}}\right\rfloor,

with
\tau
,
\iota
defined in (
C.15
), (
C.16
),
such that: (i) the average loss over iterations
[T^{\dagger},T]
decreased to
2\epsilon
, i.e.

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T-T^{\dagger}+1}\sum_{s=T^{\dagger}}^{T}L(\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\leq 2\epsilon,

(C.7)

(ii) the model does not learn weak signal
\mathbf{v}
well enough, compared to initialization, i.e.

\displaystyle\max_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle\right|\leq 2\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}.

(C.8)

In the small learning rate regime, the dynamics go through two stages, in which the strong signal
\mathbf{u}
will be firstly learned exponentially fast, and subsequently fully fit the given data point
(\mathbf{x},y)
therefore stabilizing the training process.
The following lemma plays an important role in the exponentially increasing stage, for which we single it out here.

Lemma C.4

(Derivative lower bound)
.

For any
0<\tau<1
to be tuned later, suppose at some time
t
there holds

\displaystyle\max_{r\in[m],j\in\{\pm 1\}}\left\{\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}\rangle\right|,\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle\right|\right\}\leq\sqrt{\frac{\tau}{2}},

(C.9)

then we can lower bound the fitting residual by
-y\ell^{(t)}\geq 1-\tau
.

Proof of Lemma
C.4
.

Plug into the CNN model definition (
3.1
), we have that

\displaystyle-y\ell^{(t)}=1-F_{y}(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})+F_{-y}(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\geq 1-F_{y}(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)}).

(C.10)

We can upper bound
F_{y}(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})
further by

\displaystyle F_{y}(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)},y\mathbf{u}\rangle)+\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)},y\mathbf{v}\rangle)

(C.11)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{r\in[m]}\left\{\langle\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)},y\mathbf{u}\rangle^{2}+\langle\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)},y\mathbf{v}\rangle^{2}\right\}

(C.12)

\displaystyle\leq\tau.

(C.13)

Then it follows that
-y\ell^{(t)}\geq 1-\tau
.
∎

C.1
Stage 1. Exponential Growth

We will mainly track the maximal inner product between
\mathbf{w}
and the signal vectors
\mathbf{v}
and
\mathbf{u}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\Psi^{(t)}=\max_{j,r}\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle\right|,\quad\Phi^{(t)}=\max_{j,r}\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}\rangle\right|.

(C.14)

In the following, we would take

\displaystyle\tau

\displaystyle=\max\left\{2\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}\left(2\log(16m/p)\right)^{1/2-\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}/4\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}},1-\frac{6\sqrt{2}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}}{\|\mathbf{u}\|^{2}_{2}\log(2/\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)})}\right\},

(C.15)

\displaystyle\iota

\displaystyle=\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}\cdot\exp\left\{\frac{1-\tau}{6}\log\left(\frac{\sqrt{\tau/2}}{\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}}\right)\right\}.

(C.16)

By the conditions in Assumption
C.2
on
\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}/\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}
, we find
\tau,\iota
both constants in
(0,1)
.

Lemma C.5

(First stage: one training data case)
.

Under the same conditions as Theorem
C.3
, there exists time

\displaystyle T^{\dagger}=\frac{m}{\eta(1-\tau)\|\mathbf{u}\|^{2}_{2}}\log\left(\frac{2\iota}{\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}}\right),

(C.17)

such that:
(i) the model learns the strong signal to a constant level, i.e.,

\displaystyle\max_{r\in[m]}\langle\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(T^{\dagger})},y\mathbf{u}\rangle\geq\iota,

(C.18)

(ii) compared to the random initialization, the model does not learn weak signal that much, i.e.,

\displaystyle\max_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(T^{\dagger})},\mathbf{v}\rangle\right|\leq 2\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}.

(C.19)

Proof of Lemma
C.5
.

Firstly, we would find
\{\Psi^{(t)},\Phi^{(t)}\}_{t\geq 0}
having an exponentially growing upper bound. Recursively, we would have that

\displaystyle\Psi^{(t+1)}

\displaystyle\leq\Psi^{(t)}+\max_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\left|\frac{jy\eta}{m}\cdot\big(f(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})-y\big)\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y\mathbf{v}\rangle)\cdot\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}\right|

(C.20)

\displaystyle=\Psi^{(t)}+\frac{\eta}{m}\cdot\left|\ell^{(t)}\right|\cdot\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\max_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y\mathbf{v}\rangle)

(C.21)

\displaystyle\leq\Psi^{(t)}+\frac{2\eta}{m}\cdot\left|\ell^{(t)}\right|\cdot\|\mathbf{v}\|^{2}_{2}\cdot\Psi^{(t)}

(C.22)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(\frac{6\eta\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\right)\cdot\Psi^{(t)}.

(C.23)

Therefore, we have that

\displaystyle\Psi^{(t)}\leq\exp\left(\frac{6\eta\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}t}{m}\right)\cdot\Psi^{(0)}\leq\exp\left(\frac{6\eta\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}t}{m}\right)\cdot\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}.

(C.24)

It follows by the same argument that that

\displaystyle\Phi^{(t)}\leq\exp\left(\frac{6\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|^{2}_{2}t}{m}\right)\Phi^{(0)}\leq\exp\left(\frac{6\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}t}{m}\right)\cdot\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}.

(C.25)

Note that the growing rates of these two bounds differ a lot due to the different magnitudes of
\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}
and
\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}
.
Our subsequent analysis illustrates that
\Phi^{(t)}
can grow into a constant-level magnitude since the strong signal
\mathbf{u}
is significant enough.
Now we can track how well our model learns
\mathbf{u}
by

\displaystyle A^{(t)}=\max_{r\in[m]}\langle\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)},y\mathbf{u}\rangle.

(C.26)

By the definition,
A^{(t)}\leq\Phi^{(t)}
also admits an exponentially growing upper bound. For a certain
\tau\in(0,1)
, due to the previous upper bounds (
C.24
) and (
C.25
),
\max\{\Phi^{(t)},\Psi^{(t)}\}\leq\sqrt{\tau/2}
remains true at least until

\displaystyle T_{1}=\frac{m}{6\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|^{2}}\log\left(\frac{\sqrt{\tau/2}}{\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}}\right).

(C.27)

Consequently, until at least
T_{1}
, we can use Lemma
C.4
to conclude that
-y\ell^{(t)}\geq 1-\tau
, which enables lower bounding
A^{(t)}
.
Specifically, start with the updating rule

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t+1)},y\mathbf{u}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)},y\mathbf{u}\rangle+\frac{\eta}{m}\cdot\big(-y\ell^{(t)}\big)\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)},y\mathbf{u}\rangle)\cdot\|\mathbf{u}\|^{2}_{2}

(C.28)

\displaystyle\geq\langle\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)},y\mathbf{u}\rangle+\frac{2\eta(1-\tau)\|\mathbf{u}\|^{2}_{2}}{m}\cdot\max_{r\in[m]}\left\{\langle\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)},y\mathbf{u}\rangle,0\right\},

(C.29)

and take maximum over
r\in[m]
to see that

\displaystyle A^{(t+1)}\geq A^{(t)}+\frac{2\eta(1-\tau)\|\mathbf{u}\|^{2}_{2}}{m}\cdot A^{(t)}\geq\exp\left(\frac{\eta(1-\tau)\|\mathbf{u}\|^{2}_{2}}{m}\right)\cdot A^{(t)},

(C.30)

where the last equality is by
1+z\geq\exp(z/2)
for any
0\leq z\leq 2
.
Consequently, we would have

\displaystyle A^{(t)}\geq\exp\left(\frac{\eta(1-\tau)\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}t}{m}\right)\cdot A^{(0)}\geq\exp\left(\frac{\eta(1-\tau)\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}t}{m}\right)\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}/2,

(C.31)

at least until
t\leq T_{1}
defined in (
C.27
).
Then we define another time

\displaystyle T_{2}=\frac{m}{\eta(1-\tau)\|\mathbf{u}\|^{2}_{2}}\log\left(\frac{2\iota}{\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}}\right)\leq T_{1},

(C.32)

where the inequality is due to the scaling of
\iota
upon
\tau
. Plugging
T_{2}
into the exponential lower bound (
C.31
), we can conclude that

\displaystyle\Phi^{(T_{2})}\geq A^{(T_{2})}\geq\iota,

(C.33)

which already grows up to a constant level magnitude by the time
T_{2}
. Lastly, we plug the definition of
T_{2}
to upper bound
\Psi^{(T_{2})}
as

\Psi^{(T_{2})}\leq\exp\left(\frac{6\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}}{(1-\tau)\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{2\iota}{\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}}\right)\right)\cdot\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}\leq 2\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}.

In conclusion, by taking
T^{\dagger}=T_{2}
, this lemma is completely proved.
∎

C.2
Stage 2. Stabilized Convergence

In the second stage, our lemmas would suggest that before the model really learns the weak signal
\mathbf{v}
(i.e. before
\max_{j,r}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|
breaks the
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2})
upper bound), the model already fits the given data point by exploiting the strong signal
\mathbf{u}
and decreasing the loss to
\epsilon
.

Lemma C.6

(Second stage: one training data case)
.

Under the same conditions as Theorem
C.3
, for any
\epsilon=0.01
, there exists time

T=T^{\dagger}+\left\lfloor\frac{Cm^{3}}{2\eta\epsilon\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}\right\rfloor

such that: (i) the average loss over iterations within this stage has decreased to

2\epsilon
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T-T^{\dagger}+1}\sum_{s=T^{\dagger}}^{T}L(\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\leq 2\epsilon,

(C.34)

(ii) throughout the training dynamics
0\leq t\leq T
, there holds

\displaystyle\max_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle|\leq 2\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}.

(C.35)

The proof of Lemma
C.6
relies on the following three lemmas (Lemmas
C.7
,
C.8
, and
C.9
).
We present these lemmas with proofs and then combine them to give the proof of Lemma
C.6
.

Firstly, we identify when the upper bound on
\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle
breaks and find that the conclusions of Lemma
C.5
still holds before that time.

Lemma C.7
.

Under the same conditions in Theorem
C.3
, take
\eta\leq m/6\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}
. There exists a time

\displaystyle T^{\ddagger}=\frac{m}{6\eta\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}}\log(2)\geq T^{\dagger}

(C.36)

such that

\displaystyle\max_{r\in[m]}\langle\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)},y\mathbf{u}\rangle\geq\iota/2,\quad\max_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle\right|\leq 2\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}.

(C.37)

hold for any
T^{\dagger}\leq t\leq T^{\ddagger}
.

Proof of Lemma
C.7
.

Firstly, we need to adopt the exponential upper bound derived in proving Lemma
C.5
,

\displaystyle\Psi^{(t)}\leq\exp\left(\frac{6\eta\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}t}{m}\right)\cdot\Psi^{(0)}\leq\exp\left(\frac{6\eta\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}t}{m}\right)\cdot\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}.

(C.38)

Then we naturally find that before
T^{\ddagger}
, it would always hold that

\displaystyle\max_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle\right|\leq 2\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}.

(C.39)

Due to the conditions on
\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}/\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}
,
T^{\ddagger}
is found to be much larger than
T^{\dagger}
.
Then we proceed by induction to prove the other assertion.
At time
t=T^{\dagger}
, the lower bound
\max_{r}\langle\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)},y\mathbf{u}\rangle\geq\iota/2
holds as a consequence of the previous lemma.
Suppose it holds until time
t
.
We restate the updating rule by

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t+1)},y\mathbf{u}\rangle=\langle\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)},y\mathbf{u}\rangle+\frac{\eta}{m}\cdot\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\big)\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)},y\mathbf{u}\rangle)\cdot\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2},

(C.40)

from which we find
\max_{r\in[m]}\langle\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t+1)},y\mathbf{u}\rangle\geq\max_{r\in[m]}\langle\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)},y\mathbf{u}\rangle
must hold when
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\leq 1
. Otherwise, once
yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})>1
, it immediately follows that

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle<yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})=F_{y}(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})-F_{-y}(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})

(C.41)

\displaystyle\leq F_{y}(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}^{(t)}_{y,r},y\mathbf{u}\rangle)+\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}^{(t)}_{y,r},y\mathbf{v}\rangle)

(C.42)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{r\in[m]}\langle\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)},y\mathbf{u}\rangle^{2}+2\log(16m/p)\cdot\sigma_{0}^{2}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}.

(C.43)

Consequently, for the specific neuron
r^{\ast}=\mathop{\mathrm{argmax}}_{r\in[m]}\langle\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)},y\mathbf{u}\rangle
, there holds

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{y,r^{\ast}}^{(t+1)},y\mathbf{u}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\langle\mathbf{w}_{y,r^{\ast}}^{(t)},y\mathbf{u}\rangle-\frac{3\eta}{m}\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{y,r^{\ast}}^{(t)},y\mathbf{u}\rangle\cdot\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}

(C.44)

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-2\log(16m/p)\cdot\sigma_{0}^{2}\|\mathbf{v}\|^{2}_{2}\right)\cdot\left(1-\frac{3\eta}{m}\cdot\|\mathbf{u}\|^{2}_{2}\right)

(C.45)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\iota}{2},

(C.46)

where the last inequality is enabled by taking
\eta\leq m/6\|\mathbf{u}\|^{2}_{2}
and
\sigma_{0}\leq\sqrt{1-\iota}/(2\log(16m/p)\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2})
.
Thus by induction, we have finished the proof of Lemma
C.7
.
∎

Our subsequently analysis confirms that even before
T^{\ddagger}
, the model can already fit the given data point by exploiting
\mathbf{u}
.
For the given
0<\epsilon<1
, define a reference point
\mathbf{W}^{\ast}
as

\displaystyle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{\ast}=\frac{4m(1+\epsilon)}{\iota}\cdot\frac{j\mathbf{u}}{\|\mathbf{u}\|^{2}_{2}},\quad j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m].

(C.47)

Lemma C.8
.

Under the same condition as the previous lemma, for all
T^{\dagger}\leq t\leq T^{\ddagger}
, there holds that

\displaystyle y\langle\nabla f(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)}),\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\rangle\geq 2\cdot(1+\epsilon).

(C.48)

Proof of Lemma
C.8
.

Recall the definition of the CNN in (
3.1
) and that
\mathbf{u}\perp\mathbf{v}
, so we have

\displaystyle y\langle\nabla f(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)}),\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j}^{(t)},y\mathbf{u}\rangle)\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{\ast},y\mathbf{u}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y\mathbf{u}\rangle)\cdot\frac{4(1+\epsilon)}{\iota}

\displaystyle\geq\max_{r\in[m]}\langle\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)},y\mathbf{u}\rangle\cdot\frac{4(1+\epsilon)}{\iota}

\displaystyle\geq 2\cdot(1+\epsilon),

where the last inequality is by
\max_{r\in[m]}\langle\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)},y\mathbf{u}\rangle\geq\iota/2
as shown by the previous lemma.
∎

Lemma C.9
.

Continued from the previous setting, we know that for
T^{\dagger}\leq t\leq T^{\ddagger}
, it holds that

\|\mathbf{W}^{(t)}-\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\|_{F}^{2}-\|\mathbf{W}^{(t+1)}-\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\|_{F}^{2}\geq 2\eta L(\mathbf{W}^{(t)})-2\eta\epsilon^{2}.

Proof of Lemma
C.9
.

Firstly we expand the difference by

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{W}^{(t)}-\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\|_{F}^{2}-\|\mathbf{W}^{(t+1)}-\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\|_{F}^{2}=2\eta\cdot\langle\nabla L(\mathbf{W}^{(t}),\mathbf{W}^{(t)}-\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\rangle-\eta^{2}\cdot\|\nabla L(\mathbf{W}^{(t})\|_{F}^{2}.

(C.49)

With one data point,
\nabla L(\mathbf{W}^{(t)})=\ell^{(t)}\nabla f(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})
admits a simplified expression, where
\ell^{(t)}=f(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x})-y
denotes the fitting residual.
Since the neural network
f(\mathbf{W},\mathbf{x})
is
2
-homogeneous in
\mathbf{W}
due to the activation function
\sigma(z)=\max\{z,0\}^{2}
, we can have

\displaystyle\langle\nabla f(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)}),\mathbf{W}^{(t)}\rangle=2f(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)}).

(C.50)

Stack these observations into the first term of previous difference expansion to obtain that

\displaystyle\langle\nabla L(\mathbf{W}^{(t}),\mathbf{W}^{(t)}-\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\rangle

\displaystyle=\ell^{(t)}\cdot\langle\nabla f(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)}),\mathbf{W}^{(t)}-\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\rangle

\displaystyle=\ell^{(t)}\cdot\big(2f(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})-\langle\nabla f(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)}),\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\rangle\big)

\displaystyle=2\ell^{(t)}\cdot\big(f(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})-y\big)+\ell^{(t)}\cdot y\cdot\big(2-y\langle\nabla f(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)}),\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\rangle\big).

Note that the first term is exactly
4L(\mathbf{W}^{(t)})
. As for the second term, we need to plug in Lemma
C.8
to see
2-y\langle\nabla f(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)}),\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\rangle\leq-2\epsilon<0
, so that

\displaystyle\left|\ell^{(t)}\cdot y\cdot\big(2-y\langle\nabla f(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)}),\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\rangle\big)\right|\leq\frac{1}{2}\big(\ell^{(t)}\big)^{2}+2\epsilon^{2}=L(\mathbf{W}^{(t)})+2\epsilon^{2}.

As a result, we would know
\langle\nabla L(\mathbf{W}^{(t}),\mathbf{W}^{(t)}-\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\rangle\geq 3L(\mathbf{W}^{(t)})-2\epsilon^{2}
. Next, an upper bound on the second order term
\eta^{2}\cdot\|\nabla L(\mathbf{W}^{(t})\|_{F}^{2}
is given by

\displaystyle\eta^{2}\cdot\|\nabla L(\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\eta^{2}\cdot\big(\ell^{(t)}\big)^{2}\cdot\left(\|\mathbf{u}\|^{2}_{2}\sum_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}^{(t)}_{j,r},y\mathbf{u}\rangle)+\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}\sum_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}^{(t)}_{j,r},y\mathbf{v}\rangle)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{O}\big(\max\{\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2},\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}\}\big)\cdot\eta^{2}\cdot L(\mathbf{W}^{(t}),

since the dynamics of the inner products
\langle\mathbf{w}^{(t)}_{j,r},y\mathbf{u}\rangle,\langle\mathbf{w}^{(t)}_{j,r},y\mathbf{v}\rangle
are well bounded by
\mathcal{O}(1)
throughout the time we are considering.
By scaling
\eta\mathcal{O}(\max\{\|\mathbf{u}\|^{2},\|\mathbf{v}\|^{2}\})\leq 1
, we would know that
\eta^{2}\|\nabla L(\mathbf{W}^{(t})\|_{F}^{2}\leq\eta L(\mathbf{W}^{(t})
. Eventually, continued from (
C.49
), we can completely prove this lemma.
∎

Equipped with Lemmas
C.7
,
C.8
, and
C.9
, we are ready to prove the main lemma for the second stage.

Proof of Lemma
C.6
.

Continued from Lemma
C.9
, for any
t\geq T^{\dagger}
, it holds that

\frac{1}{t-T^{\dagger}+1}\sum_{s=T^{\dagger}}^{t}L(\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\leq\frac{\|\mathbf{W}^{(T^{\dagger})}-\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\|_{F}^{2}}{2\eta(t-T^{\dagger}+1)}+\epsilon^{2}.

Before proceeding to scale time
t
, it would be helpful to decompose
\|\mathbf{W}^{(T^{\dagger})}-\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\|_{F}^{2}
and to have an upper bound on this term,

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{W}^{(T^{\dagger})}-\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\|_{F}^{2}

(C.51)

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(T^{\dagger})}-\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{\ast},\mathbf{u}\rangle^{2}}{\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}+\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(T^{\dagger})}-\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{\ast},\mathbf{v}\rangle^{2}}{\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}}+\left\|\left(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\frac{\mathbf{v}\mathbf{v}^{\top}}{\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}}-\frac{\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{\top}}{\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}\right)(\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(T^{\dagger})}-\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{\ast})\right\|_{2}^{2}

(C.52)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\sum_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\frac{2\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(T^{\dagger})},\mathbf{u}\rangle^{2}+2\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{\ast},\mathbf{u}\rangle^{2}}{\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}+\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(T^{\dagger})},\mathbf{v}\rangle^{2}}{\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}}+\left\|\left(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\frac{\mathbf{v}\mathbf{v}^{\top}}{\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}}-\frac{\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{\top}}{\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}\right)\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(0)}\right\|_{2}^{2},

(C.53)

where we exploit the fact that
\mathbf{w}^{\ast}
is parallel to
\mathbf{u}
by Lemma (
C.47
), and the gradient steps only updates
\mathbf{w}
along the directions of
\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}
.
Recall that by Lemma
C.7
,

\displaystyle\max_{j\in\pm 1,r\in[m]}\langle\mathbf{w}^{(T^{\dagger})}_{j,r},j\mathbf{u}\rangle=\Omega(1),\quad\max_{j\in\pm 1,r\in[m]}\left|\langle\mathbf{w}^{(T^{\dagger})}_{j,r},\mathbf{v}\rangle\right|=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}),

(C.54)

and also that
\|\mathbf{w}^{(0)}_{j,r}\|=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\sigma_{0}\sqrt{d})
, the leading term in (
C.53
) would be
\sum_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{\ast},\mathbf{u}\rangle^{2}/\|\mathbf{u}\|^{2}_{2}
.
Therefore, we conclude that
\|\mathbf{W}^{(T^{\dagger})}-\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\|_{F}^{2}\leq Cm^{3}/\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}
for some constant
C>0
.
As a result, the average loss after iterations
T^{\dagger}
can be bounded by

\frac{1}{t-T^{\dagger}+1}\sum_{s=T^{\dagger}}^{t}L(\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\leq\frac{Cm^{3}}{2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}(t-T^{\dagger}+1)}+\epsilon^{2},\quad\forall t^{\dagger}\leq t\leq T^{\ddagger}.

Then we choose time
T=T^{\dagger}+\lfloor Cm^{3}/(2\eta\epsilon\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2})\rfloor
as stated in Lemma
C.6
.
Since
\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}/\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}\geq\widetilde{\Omega}(m^{2})
by Assumption
C.2
, we can verify that
T\leq T^{\ddagger}
.
In conclusion, the final output would be

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T-T^{\dagger}+1}\sum_{s=T^{\dagger}}^{T}L(\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\leq\epsilon+\epsilon^{2}\leq 2\epsilon.

(C.55)

This finishes the proof of Lemma
C.6
.
∎

Combine Lemmas
C.5
and
C.6
to obtain the full version of Theorem
C.3
.

Appendix D
Proofs for Main Theoretical Results (Section
4
)

In this section, we give a detailed proof for our main theoretical results for the multiple training data case, i.e., Theorem
4.3
.
The proofs follow the similar idea as the proofs for single training data case (Appendix
B
).
The readers interested in the proofs are encouraged to first go through Appendix
B
to get the idea of the core steps.
In Appendix
D.1
, we give a preliminary analysis of the SGD training dynamics.
In Appendix
D.2
, we give an overview of the proofs with our fundamental reasoning towards weak signal learning.
In Appendix
D.3
, we give the proof of Theorem
4.3
.
We prove other lemmas in subsequent sections.

D.1
Preliminary Analysis

Recall that
\mathcal{W}
is the index set of training data points which lack the strong feature patch.
By Equation (
2.5
), the CNN weights are updated according to

\displaystyle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t+1)}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)}-\frac{j\eta}{m}\cdot\big(f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})-y_{i_{t}}\big)\cdot\left(\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y_{i_{t}}\mathbf{u}\rangle)\cdot y_{i_{t}}\mathbf{u}\cdot\mathbf{1}\{i_{t}\notin\mathcal{W}\}\right.

(D.1)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y_{i_{t}}\mathbf{v}\rangle)\cdot y_{i_{t}}\mathbf{v}+\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)}\cdot\bm{\xi}_{i_{t}}\rangle)\cdot\bm{\xi}_{i_{t}}+\left.\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)}\cdot\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i_{t}}\rangle)\cdot\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i_{t}}\cdot\mathbf{1}\{i_{t}\in\mathcal{W}\}\right).

(D.2)

Also, recall that the correct index sets for the strong and weak signal patches are defined as

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{j,+}^{(t)}:=\left\{r\in[m]:\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},j\mathbf{u}\rangle\geq 0\right\},\quad\mathcal{V}_{j,+}^{(t)}:=\left\{r\in[m]:\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},j\mathbf{v}\rangle\geq 0\right\},

(D.3)

By the CNN expression (
2.3
), for each
j\in\{\pm 1\}
, the inner products that matter are

1.

positive neurons:

\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},j\mathbf{u}\rangle
for
r\in\mathcal{U}_{j,+}^{(t)}
,
\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},j\mathbf{v}\rangle
for
r\in\mathcal{V}_{j,+}^{(t)}
;

2.

negative neurons:

\langle\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t)},j\mathbf{u}\rangle
for
r\notin\mathcal{U}_{-j,+}^{(t)}
,
\langle\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t)},j\mathbf{v}\rangle
for
r\notin\mathcal{V}_{-j,+}^{(t)}
.

By (
D.2
), the update formula of these inner products of interests are given by

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t+1)},j\mathbf{u}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},j\mathbf{u}\rangle+\frac{\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\big(1-y_{i_{t}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\big)\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},j\mathbf{u}\rangle jy_{i_{t}})\cdot\mathbf{1}\{i_{t}\notin\mathcal{W}\},

(D.4)

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t+1)},j\mathbf{v}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},j\mathbf{v}\rangle+\frac{\eta\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\big(1-y_{i_{t}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\big)\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},j\mathbf{v}\rangle jy_{i_{t}}),

(D.5)

Also by (
D.2
), the update formula of the inner products with noise vectors are given by

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t+1)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle+\frac{\eta\cdot jy_{i_{t}}}{m}\cdot\big(1-y_{i_{t}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\big)\cdot\left(\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i_{t}}\rangle)\cdot\langle\bm{\xi}_{i_{t}},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\right.

(D.6)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left.\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i_{t}}\rangle)\cdot\langle\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i_{t}},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\cdot\mathbf{1}\{i_{t}\in\mathcal{W}\}\right),\quad i\in[n],

(D.7)

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t+1)},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\frac{\eta\cdot jy_{i_{t}}}{m}\cdot\big(1-y_{i_{t}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\big)\cdot\left(\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i_{t}}\rangle)\cdot\langle\bm{\xi}_{i_{t}},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right.

(D.8)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left.\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i_{t}}\rangle)\cdot\langle\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i_{t}},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\cdot\mathbf{1}\{i_{t}\in\mathcal{W}\}\right),\quad i\in\mathcal{W}.

(D.9)

At first glance, the update formulas given above seem intangible. The following proposition indicates that we can separate the neurons into two parts, with each individual part learning one kind of sample independently.
For simplicity, we let
\tilde{\eta}\coloneqq 2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|^{2}_{2}/m
and
\alpha=\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}/\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}
and
\tilde{f}_{t}=y_{{i_{t}}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})
.
For any
j\in\{\pm 1\}
and
s\geq 0
, we define effective running time for learning label-
j
-samples as

\displaystyle t_{j}(s)=\min\big\{t\in\mathbb{N}:t>t_{j}(s-1),\;y_{i_{t}}=j\big\},

(D.10)

with
t_{j}(0)=\min\{t\in\mathbb{N}:y_{i_{t}}=j\}
.

Proposition D.1
.

Suppose that the sign stability condition holds before some
T_{\mathrm{sign}}
, i.e.,
\mathcal{U}^{(t)}_{\pm j,\pm}=\mathcal{U}^{(0)}_{\pm j,\pm}:=\mathcal{U}_{\pm j,\pm}
and
\mathcal{V}^{(t)}_{\pm j,\pm}=\mathcal{V}^{(0)}_{\pm j,\pm}:=\mathcal{V}_{\pm j,\pm}
for
t\leq T_{\mathrm{sign}}
. Then for
s
such that
t_{j}(s)\leq T_{\mathrm{sign}}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(s+1))}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(s))}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\cdot\big(1+\tilde{\eta}(1-\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(s)})\cdot\mathbf{1}\{i_{t_{j}(s)}\in\mathcal{W}\}\big),\quad\forall r\in\mathcal{U}_{j,+};

(D.11)

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t_{j}(s+1))}},{-j\mathbf{u}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t_{j}(s))}},{-j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\cdot\big(1-\tilde{\eta}(1-\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(s)})\cdot\mathbf{1}\{i_{t_{j}(s)}\in\mathcal{W}\}\big),\quad\forall r\in\mathcal{U}_{-j,-};

(D.12)

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(s+1))}},{j\mathbf{v}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(s))}},{j\mathbf{v}}\rangle\cdot\big(1+\alpha\tilde{\eta}(1-\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(s)})\big),\quad\forall r\in\mathcal{V}_{j,+};

(D.13)

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t_{j}(s+1))}},{-j\mathbf{v}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t_{j}(s))}},{-j\mathbf{v}}\rangle\cdot\big(1-\alpha\tilde{\eta}(1-\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(s)})\big),\quad\forall r\in\mathcal{V}_{-j,-}.

(D.14)

Moreover, for every
t\in(t_{j}(s),t_{j}(s+1)]
, it holds that

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(s)+1)}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle,\quad\forall r\in\mathcal{U}_{j,+};

(D.15)

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t)}},{-j\mathbf{u}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t_{j}(s)+1)}},{-j\mathbf{u}}\rangle,\quad\forall r\in\mathcal{U}_{-j,-};

(D.16)

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)}},{j\mathbf{v}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(s)+1)}},{j\mathbf{v}}\rangle,\quad\forall r\in\mathcal{V}_{j,+};

(D.17)

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t)}},{-j\mathbf{v}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t_{j}(s)+1)}},{-j\mathbf{v}}\rangle,\quad\forall r\in\mathcal{V}_{-j,-}.

(D.18)

Proof of Proposition
D.1
.

See Appendix
D.6.1
for a detailed proof.
∎

This proposition helps to break down the dynamics of multiple data training into two folds. To see this, it suffices to note that the following components

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(s)},\quad\left\{\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\right\}_{r\in\mathcal{U}_{j,+}},\quad\left\{\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t)}},{-j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\right\}_{r\in\mathcal{U}_{-j,-}},\quad\left\{\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)}},{j\mathbf{v}}\rangle\right\}_{r\in\mathcal{V}_{j,+}},\quad\left\{\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t)}},{-j\mathbf{v}}\rangle\right\}_{r\in\mathcal{V}_{-j,+}}

(D.19)

are independent with
\{\tilde{f}_{t}\}_{i_{t}=-j}
and the rest inner products associated with
-j
.

D.2
Overview of Analysis
