The roadmap towards proving our main theorem shares nearly the same logic with the proof of single data setup (Appendix
B
).
Basically, as long as the weak signal component and the noise component are not learned in the sense that these inner products remain negligible, we can prove that the inner products associated with the strong signal would dominate and the oscillation would accumulate at a linear rate.
This further gives Lemma
D.3
which shows the CNN would learn effectively learn the weak signal
\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},j\mathbf{v}\rangle
.
Meanwhile, we can prove that the influences from the negative part of weak signal learning
\langle\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t)},j\mathbf{v}\rangle
and the noise memorization
\langle\mathbf{w}_{\pm 1,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle
,
\langle\mathbf{w}_{\pm 1,r}^{(t)},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
can be well controlled (Propositions
D.4
).
Putting all together, we can prove the main result Theorem
4.3
.

To be formal, we define two important stopping times as follows:

\displaystyle T^{j}_{(\mathbf{v})}

\displaystyle=\min_{t\geq 0}\left\{t:\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},j\mathbf{v}\rangle)\geq\delta/2\right\},

(D.20)

\displaystyle T_{(\bm{\xi})}

\displaystyle=\min_{t\geq 0}\left\{t:\max_{r\in[m],j\in\{\pm 1\}}\left\{\max_{i\in[n]}\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\right|,\max_{i\in\mathcal{W}}\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|\right\}\geq\delta/4\right\}.

(D.21)

We recap that
\mathcal{W}
denotes the index set of weak data, and
\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}
denotes the Gaussian noise appearing on the lacking strong signal patch for those weak data.
Also we note that
T^{j}_{(\mathbf{v})}
,
T_{(\bm{\xi})}\leq+\infty
, where the equal sign is attainable.
We then define

\displaystyle T_{\max}^{j}=\min\left\{T^{j}_{(\mathbf{v})},T_{(\bm{\xi})}\right\}.

(D.22)

In the first place, following the same arguments as in the single data setup (Appendix
B
), we can derive the boundedness and sign stability results before time
T_{\max}^{j}
.

Lemma D.2

(Boundedness and sign stability)
.

Under Assumptions
4.1
and
4.2
, for fixed
j\in\{\pm 1\}
, the followings hold with probability at least
1-p=1-1/\mathrm{poly}(d)
:

1.

it holds that
\mathcal{U}_{j,+}^{(t)}=\mathcal{U}_{j,+}^{(0)}\neq\emptyset
and
\mathcal{V}_{j,+}^{(t)}=\mathcal{V}_{j,+}^{(0)}\neq\emptyset
for any
t\in[0,T_{\max}^{j}]
. Hence the superscript
(t)
can be dropped;

2.

for any
t\in[0,T_{\max}^{j}]
, we have that

\displaystyle\max_{r\in[m]}\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq 1.5\cdot(1.05\beta_{\mathbf{u},j}^{*}m)^{1/2},

(D.23)

where
\beta_{\mathbf{u},j}^{*}
is defined in Lemma
B.5
;

3.

for any
t\in[0,T_{\max}^{j}]
it holds that

\displaystyle\min_{r\in[m]}\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t)}},{-j\mathbf{u}}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq-{\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}},

(D.24)

\displaystyle\min_{r\in[m]}\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t)}},{-j\mathbf{v}}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq-{\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}};

(D.25)

4.

for any
t\in[0,T_{\max}^{j}]
such that
y_{i_{t}}=j
, it holds that

\displaystyle\big|1-y_{i_{t}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\big|\leq 2.

(D.26)

Proof of Lemma
D.2
.

See Appendix
D.4
for a detailed proof.
∎

This boundedness and sign stability result further implies weak signal learning with an exponential rate, which is formally presented as follows.

Lemma D.3

(Weak signal learning)
.

Under Assumptions
4.1
and
4.2
, with probability at least
1-1/\mathrm{poly}(d)
, it holds that for any
j\in\{\pm 1\}
and
0\leq t_{1}\leq t_{2}\leq T_{\max}^{j}
that

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s=t_{1}\\
y_{i_{s}}=j\end{subarray}}^{t_{2}}\big(1-y_{i_{s}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{s}};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)\geq\frac{\delta}{16}\cdot\big(1-(1.05-\delta/4)^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)\cdot(t_{2}-t_{1}+1)-\frac{m{(1.05)^{\frac{1}{2}}}}{2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}(1.05-\delta/4)^{\frac{1}{2}}},

(D.27)

where
\delta
is specified in Assumption
4.2
. Moreover, for
r\in\mathcal{V}_{j,+}^{(0)}
, we have that

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{2}+1)}},{j\mathbf{v}}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{1})}},{j\mathbf{v}}\rangle\!\cdot\!\exp\left\{\frac{\eta\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}}{32m}\!\cdot\!\big(\delta-\delta(1.05-\delta/4)^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)\!\cdot\!(t_{2}-t_{1}+1)-\frac{\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}{(1.05)^{\frac{1}{2}}}}{\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}(1.05-\delta/4)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right\}.

(D.28)

Proof of Lemma
D.3
.

See Appendix
D.5
for a detailed proof.
∎

The last component to complete our proof of main theorem is controlling noise memorization during the training process.
For simplicity, we define the maximum absolute value of the noise inner products over data as

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{(t)}=\max_{r\in[m],j\in\{\pm 1\}}\left\{\max_{i\in[n]}\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\right|,\max_{i\in\mathcal{W}}\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|\right\}.

(D.29)

We have the following proposition to control the growth of
\Upsilon^{(t)}
.

Proposition D.4

(Noise memorization)
.

Under Assumptions
4.1
and
4.2
, then with probability at least
1-1/\mathrm{poly}(d)
, it holds for any
0\leq t_{1}\leq\min_{j\in\{\pm 1\}}\{T_{\max}^{j}\}-\widetilde{T}_{(\bm{\xi})}
and
j\in\{\pm 1\}
that

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{(t)}\leq\Upsilon^{(t_{1})}\cdot(1+\epsilon),\quad\forall r\in[m],\quad\forall t_{1}\leq t\leq t_{1}+\widetilde{T}_{(\bm{\xi})}

(D.30)

where
\widetilde{T}_{(\bm{\xi})}=\widetilde{\Theta}(mn\cdot\eta^{-1}\cdot\epsilon\cdot(1+\epsilon)^{-1}\cdot(\sigma_{p}^{2}d)^{-1})
for any
\epsilon>0
.

Proof of Proposition
D.4
.

See Appendix
D.6.3
for a detailed proof.
∎

D.3
Proof of Theorem
4.3

With Lemma
D.2
and
D.3
and Proposition
D.4
, we are ready to prove Theorem
4.3
.

Proof of Theorem
4.3
.

For the
j=\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{j^{\prime}\in\{\pm 1\}}\{T_{\max}^{j^{\prime}}\}
, we are going to prove that
T_{\max}^{j}
is bounded by a polynomial time by using contradiction.
Specifically, we prove that

\displaystyle T_{\max}^{j}\leq T_{j,0}\coloneqq\frac{32m}{\eta\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}}\cdot\big(\delta-\delta(1.05-\delta/4)^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)^{-1}\cdot\Bigg\{\log\left(\frac{2\sqrt{m\delta}}{\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}}\right)+1.5\cdot\frac{\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}}{\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{1.05}{1.05-\delta/4}}\Bigg\}.

(D.31)

Suppose that the result fails, then by definition we have that

\displaystyle T_{\max}^{j}=T_{(\mathbf{v})}^{j}\wedge T_{(\bm{\xi})}>T_{j,0}.

(D.32)

Then Lemma
D.2
and Lemma
D.3
hold on
[0,T_{j,0}]
. By applying the lower bound in Inequality (
D.28
) as well as Lemma
A.4
, we have that

\displaystyle\max_{r\in[m]}\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(T_{j,0})}},{j\mathbf{v}}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\max_{r\in[m]}\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(0)}},{j\mathbf{v}}\rangle\cdot\exp\left\{\frac{\eta\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}}{32m}\cdot\big(\delta-\delta(1.05-\delta/4)^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)\!\cdot\!T_{j,0}-1.5\cdot\frac{\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}}{\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{1.05}{1.05-\delta/4}}\right\}

(D.33)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}\cdot\frac{2\sqrt{m\delta}}{\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}}

(D.34)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{m\delta}.

(D.35)

This leads to the following,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma(\langle{\mathbf{w}^{(T_{j,0})}_{j,r}},{j\mathbf{v}}\rangle)\geq\frac{1}{m}\cdot\max_{r\in[m]}(\langle{\mathbf{w}^{(T_{j,0})}_{j,r}},{j\mathbf{v}}\rangle)^{2}\geq\delta,

(D.36)

which clearly contradicts the definition of
T^{j}_{(\mathbf{v})}
in (
D.20
),
hence it must be that
T_{\max}^{j}\leq T_{j,0}<+\infty
.

In the following, we prove that
T_{\max}^{j}=T_{(\mathbf{v})}^{j}<T_{(\bm{\xi})}
, for which our conclusion directly follows due to (
D.20
).
Again we prove by contradiction.
Suppose that
T_{\max}^{j}=T_{(\bm{\xi})}<T_{(\mathbf{v})}^{j}
, then
\Upsilon^{(t)}
would reach
\delta/4
for time less than
T_{j,0}
due to definition of
T_{(\bm{\xi})}
in (
D.21
).
But by Proposition
D.4
with
\epsilon=1
, we know
\Upsilon^{(t)}
takes at least

\displaystyle K\widetilde{T}_{(\bm{\xi})}:=\widetilde{\Theta}\left(\frac{mn\sigma_{p}^{2}d}{\eta}\cdot\log\left(\frac{\delta}{\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d}}\right)\right)

(D.37)

steps to reach
\delta/4
, where
\widetilde{T}_{(\bm{\xi})}
is defined in Proposition
D.4
.
Then due to the scaling of the signal strength in Assumption
4.1
, we can also find that
K\widetilde{T}_{(\bm{\xi})}>T_{j,0}
, which is a contradiction with the definition of
T_{(\bm{\xi})}
.
Therefore, we must have that
T_{\max}^{j}=T_{(\mathbf{v})}^{j}
, which means that at
t^{\star}=T_{\max}^{j}
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t^{\star})},j\mathbf{v}\rangle)\geq\frac{\delta}{2}.

(D.38)

In the meanwhile, Proposition
D.2
guarantees that at time
t^{\star}=T_{\max}^{j}
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t^{\star})},j\mathbf{v}\rangle)\leq\frac{\delta}{4}.

(D.39)

Thus, we conclude that at time
t^{\star}
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t^{\star})},j\mathbf{v}\rangle)-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t^{\star})},j\mathbf{v}\rangle)\geq\frac{\delta}{4}.

(D.40)

This finishes the proof of Theorem
4.3
.
∎

D.4
Proof of Lemma
D.2

Proof of Lemma
D.2
.

Recall that
T_{\max}^{j}=T_{(\mathbf{v})}^{j}\wedge T_{(\bm{\xi})}
, where

\displaystyle T_{(\mathbf{v})}^{j}

\displaystyle=\min_{t\geq 0}\left\{t:\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)}},{j\mathbf{v}}\rangle\big)\geq\delta/2\right\},

(D.41)

\displaystyle T_{(\bm{\xi})}

\displaystyle=\min_{t\geq 0}\left\{t:\max_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m],i\in[n]}\left|\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)}},{\bm{\xi}_{i}}\rangle\right|\vee\max_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m],i\in\mathcal{W}}\left|\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)}},{\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}}\rangle\right|\geq\delta/4\right\}.

(D.42)

We now define some notations to simplify our presentation. Recall that
\tilde{f}=y_{i_{t}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})
,
\tilde{\eta}=2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}/m
and
\alpha=\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}/\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}
.
For fixed
j
, we define

\displaystyle\bar{S}_{j,k}\coloneqq\min\left\{s\in\mathbb{N}:s>\bar{S}_{j,k-1}\text{ such that }\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(s)}\geq 1\text{ and }\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(\max\{s^{\prime}<s:i_{t_{j}(s^{\prime})}\notin\mathcal{W}\})}<1\right\},

(D.43)

with
\bar{S}_{j,0}=0
, and

\displaystyle\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{j,k}\coloneqq\min\left\{s\in\mathbb{N}:s>\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{j,k-1}\text{ such that }i_{t_{j}(s)}\notin\mathcal{W},\;\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(s)}<1\text{ and }\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(\max\{s^{\prime}<s:i_{t_{j}(s^{\prime})}\notin\mathcal{W}\})}\geq 1\right\},

(D.44)

with
\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{j,0}=0
. From the definition of
T_{\max}^{j}
, we know that
i_{t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k})}\notin\mathcal{W}
for
k
such that
t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k})\leq T_{\max}^{j}
, since otherwise
\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k})}<\delta<1
.
Moreover we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{j,+}\coloneqq\left\{r\in[m]:\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(0))}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle>0\right\},\quad\mathcal{U}_{j,-}\coloneqq\left\{r\in[m]:\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(0))}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\leq 0\right\},

(D.45)

and
\mathcal{V}_{j,\pm}
are defined analogously.

The following analysis is nearly the same as the proof of one data case in the sense that each step of the proof is organized exactly the same to the proof of one data case. Therefore we suggest readers to refer to the roadmap provided in Appendix
B.1
frequently for better understanding.

We note that for
t\leq T_{(\mathbf{v})}^{j}\wedge T_{(\bm{\xi})}
, which is the time scale we are mainly focusing on, it holds that

\displaystyle\max_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m],i\in[n],i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}}\left|\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)}},{\bm{\xi}_{i}}\rangle\right|\vee\left|\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)}},{\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i^{\prime}}}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\delta}{4},\quad\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)}},{j\mathbf{v}}\rangle\big)<\frac{\delta}{2}.

(D.46)

Now we start presenting the proof of boundedness and sign stability with step-by-step analysis. The
j
is arbitrary but fixed throughout analysis.
In view of the effective running time for learning label-
j
-samples defined in (
D.10
), (
D.43
), and (
D.44
), the proofs for a certain
j
directly do induction over
s
.

Step 1: Pre-
\bar{S}_{j,1}
analysis.

Clearly at
s=0
, the lower bounds in Inequalities (
D.24
) and (
D.25
) are both guaranteed by Lemma
A.4
.
Also we know that
\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(0)}\ll 1
and so
\bar{S}_{j,1}\geq 1
. The initialization also guarantees that the upper bound in Inequality (
D.23
) holds at
s=0
.

For
s\in[0,\bar{S}_{j,1})
, the definition of
\bar{S}_{j,1}
indicates that
\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(s)}\leq 1
. Therefore, from Proposition
D.1
we can see that the
\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{t_{j}(s)}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle,\;r\in\mathcal{U}_{j,+}
and
\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{t_{j}(s)}},{-j\mathbf{u}}\rangle,\;r\in\mathcal{U}_{-j,-}
are non-decreasing in
s
during this stage. One naturally infers that for all
r\in\mathcal{U}_{-j,-}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,1}))}},{-j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\geq\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,1}-1))}},{-j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\geq\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t_{j}(0))}},{-j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\geq-{\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}}.

(D.47)

Same can be verified for
\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t_{j}(s))}},{-j\mathbf{v}}\rangle
with
r\in\mathcal{V}_{-j,-}
. Hence the lower bounds in Inequality (
D.24
) and (
D.25
) are extended to
s\in[0,\bar{S}_{j,k}]
. Also, for these inner products, the sign stability holds on
[0,\bar{S}_{j,1}]
, since
\tilde{\eta}<4/5
and thus we have

\displaystyle 1\pm\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot(1-\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(s)})

\displaystyle\geq 1-\tilde{\eta}\big(1+o(1)\big)\geq 0,

(D.48)

\displaystyle 1\pm\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot(1-\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(s)})

\displaystyle\geq 1-\alpha\tilde{\eta}\big(1+o(1)\big)\geq 0.

(D.49)

Then we turn to upper bound
\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(s))}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle
for
s\in[0,\bar{S}_{j,1})
. Note that, for
s
such that
i_{t_{j}(s)}\notin\mathcal{W}
, the definition of
T_{\max}^{j}
implies that

\displaystyle 1-\delta\geq\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(s)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(s))}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\big)+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\bigl(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(s))}},{j\mathbf{v}}\rangle\bigr)

(D.50)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(-\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t_{j}(s))}},{-j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\big)-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(-\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t_{j}(s))}},{-j\mathbf{v}}\rangle\big)

(D.51)

\displaystyle\qquad-\Big|\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(s))}},{\bm{\xi}_{i}}\rangle\big)+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t_{j}(s))}},{\bm{\xi}_{i}}\rangle\big)\Big|

(D.52)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(s))}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\big)-2\times o(1)-2\times\delta/4.

(D.53)

As a consequence,

\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(s))}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\big)\leq 1-\delta/2+o(1)\leq 1.05.

Thanks to the local sign stability, Lemma
B.5
implies that

\displaystyle\max_{r\in[m]}\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(s))}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\leq(1.05\beta^{*}_{\mathbf{u},j}m)^{1/2}.

(D.54)

If otherwise
s\in[0,\bar{S}_{j,k-1})
such that
i_{t_{j}(s)}\in\mathcal{W}
, then we choose
\tilde{s}=\min\{s^{\prime}>s:i_{t_{j}(s)}\notin\mathcal{W}\}
. It holds that either
\tilde{s}<\bar{S}_{j,1}
, or
\tilde{s}=\bar{S}_{j,1}
. For the previous case, similar argument implies that

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(s))}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\leq\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(\tilde{s}))}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\leq(1.05\beta^{*}_{\mathbf{u},j}m)^{1/2}.

(D.55)

The latter case reduces to establish the upper bound for
t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,1})
, which is derived in the next step.

Step 2.1: Bounding
\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(s))}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle
for
s\in[\bar{S}_{j,1},\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{j,1})
.

Since the weak data does not contributes to learning strong signal, as indicated in Proposition
D.1
, we know that

\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,1}))}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle=\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(\tilde{S}_{j,1}))}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\cdot\big(1+\tilde{\eta}(1-\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(\tilde{S}_{j,1})})\big),

where
\tilde{S}_{j,1}=\max\{s\leq\tilde{S}_{j,1}-1:i_{t_{j}(s)}\notin\mathcal{W}\}
.

We begin with a proposition that is parallel to Proposition
B.9
.

Proposition D.5
.

For simplicity, we assume that
i_{t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}-1)}\notin\mathcal{W}
and
\tilde{S}_{j,k}=\bar{S}_{j,k}-1
. Otherwise we can find the last step before
\bar{S}_{j,k}
such that
i_{t_{j}(s)}\notin\mathcal{W}
and leverage the previous observation in Equation (
D.4
). Suppose that

\displaystyle E_{t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}-1)}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(-\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}-1))}},{-j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\big)+\sigma\big(-\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}-1))}},{-j\mathbf{v}}\rangle\big)\leq\delta/4,

(D.56)

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{(t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}))}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\max_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m],i\in[n]}\left|\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}))}},{\bm{\xi}_{i}}\rangle\right|\vee\max_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m],i\in\mathcal{W}}\left|\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}))}},{\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}}\rangle\right|\leq\delta/4,

(D.57)

then we have that

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}-1)}\geq\frac{\tilde{\eta}+2-\sqrt{\tilde{\eta}^{2}+4\tilde{\eta}}}{2\tilde{\eta}},

(D.58)

and that

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k})}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\tilde{\eta}\big(1-\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}-1)}\big)\right)^{2}+2E_{t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}-1)}+{\Upsilon^{(t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}))}}^{2}

(D.59)

\displaystyle\leq\bigl(\tilde{\eta}/2+\sqrt{\tilde{\eta}^{2}/4+\tilde{\eta}}\bigr)^{2}+2E_{t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}-1)}+{\Upsilon^{(t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}))}}^{2}.

(D.60)

Proof of Proposition
D.5
.

See Appendix
D.6.4
for a detailed proof.
∎

With this proposition, we can derive an upper bound on
\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,1}))}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle
.
One step gradient (
D.11
) implies that, for
r\in\mathcal{U}_{j,+}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,1}))}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,1}-1))}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\cdot\left(1+\tilde{\eta}\big(1-\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,1}-1)}\big)\right)

(D.61)

\displaystyle\leq\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,1}-1))}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\cdot\left(1+\tilde{\eta}\left(1-\frac{\tilde{\eta}+2-\sqrt{\tilde{\eta}^{2}+4\tilde{\eta}}}{2\tilde{\eta}}\right)\right)

(D.62)

\displaystyle\leq 1.5\cdot(1.05\beta^{*}_{\mathbf{u},j}m)^{1/2}.

(D.63)

Here the first inequality comes from Inequality (
D.58
). Note that the upper bound on
\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,1}-1))}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle
has been derived in Inequality (
D.55
) in
Step 1.
.
Taking
\tilde{\eta}=4/5
easily implies the second inequality above.

This upper bound continues to hold for
\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(s))}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle
with
s\in[\bar{S}_{j,1},\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{j,1})
because of monotonicity. We consider the lower bound for
\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(s))}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle
with
s\in[\bar{S}_{j,1},\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{j,1})
and
i_{t_{j}(s)}\notin\mathcal{W}
. For these
s
, we have that

\displaystyle 1+\delta<\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(s)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(s))}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\big)+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(s))}},{j\mathbf{v}}\rangle\big)+(\text{negative part})+(\text{noise part})

(D.64)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(s))}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\big)+\delta/2+\delta/4.

(D.65)

Combining with Lemma
B.5
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\max_{r\in[m]}\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(s))}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\geq(\beta^{*}_{\mathbf{u},j}m)^{1/2},

(D.66)

for
s\in[\bar{S}_{j,1},\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{j,1})
and
i_{t_{j}(s)}\notin\mathcal{W}
.

Step 2.2: Lower bounding
\max_{r}\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t_{j}(\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{j,k}))}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle
.

Same to the proof of Proposition
D.5
, we can assume that
i_{t_{j}(\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{j,1}-1)}\notin\mathcal{W}
, without loss of generality.

Note that, while
\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(s)}
is not monotonically changing in
s\in[\bar{S}_{j,1},\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{j,1}]
in the presence of noise components, the inner products are still changing monotonically, as indicated in Proposition
D.1
. Therefore, we can leverage the control over noise components to derive an approximate monotonic property. Specifically, the inner products

\displaystyle\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(s))}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\big),\quad-\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t_{j}(s))}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\big),\quad\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(s))}},{j\mathbf{v}}\rangle\big),\quad-\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t_{j}(s))}},{j\mathbf{v}}\rangle\big),

(D.67)

are decreasing in
s\in[\bar{S}_{j,1},\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{j,1}]
. From Inequality (
D.153
) in the proof of Proposition
D.5
, we know that

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{j,1}-1)}

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,1})}-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,1}))}},{\bm{\xi}_{i_{t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,1})}}}\rangle\big)-\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,1}))}},{\bm{\xi}_{i_{t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,1})}}}\rangle\big)

(D.68)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{j,1}-1))}},{\bm{\xi}_{i_{t_{j}(\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{j,1}-1)}}}\rangle\big)-\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t_{j}(\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{j,1}-1))}},{\bm{\xi}_{i_{t_{j}(\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{j,1}-1)}}}\rangle\big)

(D.69)

\displaystyle\leq\big(\tilde{\eta}/2+\sqrt{\tilde{\eta}^{2}/4+\tilde{\eta}}\big)^{2}+2E_{T_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,1}-1)}+{\Upsilon^{(t_{j}(\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{j,1}))}}^{2}.

(D.70)

Recall that in
Step 1.
, we proved that
E_{j(\bar{S}_{j,1}-1)}\leq 2({\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}})^{2}=o(1)
.
Thus from doing one-step gradient descent (
D.11
), we know that for
r\in\mathcal{U}_{j,+}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{j,1}))}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{j,1}-1))}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\cdot\left(1+\tilde{\eta}\big(1-\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{j,1}-1)}\big)\right)

(D.71)

\displaystyle\geq\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{j,1}-1))}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\cdot\left(1+\tilde{\eta}\big(1-(\tilde{\eta}/2+\sqrt{\tilde{\eta}^{2}/4+\tilde{\eta}})^{2}\big)-0.1\right)

(D.72)

\displaystyle\geq 0.1\cdot(\beta^{*}_{\mathbf{u},j}m)^{1/2}.

(D.73)

Here the first inequality is by (
D.70
),
E_{j(\bar{S}_{j,1}-1)}=o(1)
and the definition of
T_{(\bm{\xi})}
, and the second inequality is a consequence of Inequality (
B.73
).
This positive constant-level lower bound guarantees that our
\tilde{\eta}
choice is sufficient for the sign stability to hold for
s\in[\bar{S}_{j,1},\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{j,2}]
, as shown in the next step.

Step 2.3: Lower bounding
\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t_{j}(\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{-j,1}))}},{-j\mathbf{u}}\rangle
and
\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t_{j}(\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{j,1}))}},{-j\mathbf{v}}\rangle
.

It suffices to consider
r\in\mathcal{V}_{-j,-}
and
r\in\mathcal{U}_{-j,-}
. Inequality (
D.73
) indicates that

\displaystyle 1\ll\frac{\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{j,1}))}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle}{\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(0))}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle}=\prod_{s=0,i_{t_{j}(s)}\notin\mathcal{W}}^{\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{j,1}-1}\left(1+\tilde{\eta}\big(1-\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(s)}\big)\right)\leq\exp\left\{\tilde{\eta}\sum_{s=0,i_{t_{j}(s)}\notin\mathcal{W}}^{\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{j,1}-1}\big(1-\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(s)}\big)\right\}.

(D.74)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\sum_{s=0,i_{t_{j}(s)}\in\mathcal{W}}^{\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{j,1}-1}\big(1-\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(s)}\big)>0.

(D.75)

Now we can prove the lower bound.
For
r\in\mathcal{U}_{-j,-}
, we have that

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t_{j}(\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{j,1}))}},{-j\mathbf{u}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t_{j}(0))}},{-j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\cdot\prod_{s=0,i_{t_{j}(s)}\notin\mathcal{W}}^{\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{j,1}-1}\left(1-\tilde{\eta}\big(1-\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(s)}\big)\right)

(D.76)

\displaystyle\geq\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t_{j}(0))}},{-j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\cdot\exp\left\{-\tilde{\eta}\sum_{s=0,i_{t_{j}(s)}\notin\mathcal{W}}^{\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{j,1}-1}\big(1-\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(s)}\big)\right\}

(D.77)

\displaystyle\geq\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t_{j}(0))}},{-j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\geq-{\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}}.

(D.78)

On the other hand, for
s\in[0,\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{j,1}-1]
such that
i_{t_{j}(s)}\in\mathcal{W}
, condition
t\leq T_{\max}^{j}=T_{(\mathbf{v})}^{j}\wedge T_{(\bm{\xi})}
guarantees that

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(s)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(s))}},{j\mathbf{v}}\rangle\big)-\sigma\big(-\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t_{j}(s))}},{-j\mathbf{v}}\rangle\big)

(D.79)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(s))}},{\bm{\xi}_{i_{t_{j}(s)}}}\rangle\big)-\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t_{j}(s))}},{\bm{\xi}_{i_{t_{j}(s)}}}\rangle\big)

(D.80)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(s))}},{\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i_{t_{j}(s)}}}\rangle\big)-\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t_{j}(s))}},{\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i_{t_{j}(s)}}}\rangle\big)

(D.81)

\displaystyle\leq\delta/2+2\times\delta/4

(D.82)

\displaystyle\leq 1.

(D.83)

Hence we derive that

\displaystyle\sum_{s\in[0,\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{j,1}-1]}\bigl(1-\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(s)}\bigr)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\in[0,\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{j,1}-1]\\
i_{t_{j}(s)}\in\mathcal{W}\end{subarray}}\bigl(1-\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(s)}\bigr)+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\in[0,\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{j,1}-1]\\
i_{t_{j}(s)}\notin\mathcal{W}\end{subarray}}\bigl(1-\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(s)}\bigr)\geq 0.

(D.84)

And in consequence, for
r\in\mathcal{V}_{j,-}
it holds that

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t_{j}(\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{j,1}))}},{-j\mathbf{v}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t_{j}(0))}},{-j\mathbf{v}}\rangle\cdot\prod_{s=0}^{\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{j,1}-1}\left(1-\alpha\tilde{\eta}\big(1-\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(s)}\big)\right)

(D.85)

\displaystyle\geq\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t_{j}(0))}},{-j\mathbf{v}}\rangle\cdot\exp\left\{-\alpha\tilde{\eta}\sum_{s=0}^{\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{j,1}-1}\big(1-\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(s)}\big)\right\}

(D.86)

\displaystyle\geq\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t_{j}(0))}},{-j\mathbf{v}}\rangle\geq-{\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}}.

(D.87)

The monotonicity again extends lower bounds in Inequalities (
D.87
) and (
D.78
) to
s\in[\bar{S}_{j,1},\bar{S}_{j,2}]
. And the sign stability naturally holds on this interval.

Step 2.4 & Finalizing.

Thanks to the sign stability proved in the last step, we can now apply Lemma
B.5
to derive that the upper bound in Inequality (
D.23
) continues to hold for all
s\in[\underaccent{\bar}{S}_{j,1},\bar{S}_{j,2}]
, with exactly the same argument as the
Step 1.
. With an inductive argument that exactly repeats the argument above, we can infer that all the results in Lemma
D.2
holds for
t_{j}(s)
with
s\in[\bar{S}_{j,1},\bar{S}_{j,2}]
. Proposition
D.1
implies that for any
t\in\mathbb{N}
we can find
s_{t}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
t_{j}(s_{t})\leq t<t_{j}(s_{t}+1)
and
\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle=\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(s_{t}))}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle
(so are all other inner products), therefore all the results in Lemma
D.2
hold for
t\leq T_{\max}^{j}
.
∎

D.5
Proof of Lemma
D.3

Proof of Lemma
D.3
.

For any
0\leq t_{1}\leq t_{2}\leq T_{\max}^{j}
,
j\in\{\pm 1\}
, and
r\in\mathcal{U}_{j,+}^{(t)}
, by (
D.4
),

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{2}+1)},j\mathbf{u}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{1})},j\mathbf{u}\rangle+\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{1}\leq s\leq t_{2}\\
y_{i_{s}}=j,i_{s}\notin\mathcal{W}\\
y_{i_{s}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{s}};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})>1\end{subarray}}\big(1-y_{i_{s}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{s}};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(s)},j\mathbf{u}\rangle

(D.88)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{1}\leq s\leq t_{2}\\
y_{i_{s}}=j,i_{s}\notin\mathcal{W}\\
y_{i_{s}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{s}};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})<1\end{subarray}}\big(1-y_{i_{s}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{s}};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(s)},j\mathbf{u}\rangle,

(D.89)

Note that for
0\leq t_{1}\leq t_{2}\leq T_{\max}^{j}
, we can apply the conclusions of Lemma
D.2
.
Specifically, we consider the maximal neuron
r^{*}=\mathop{\mathrm{argmax}}_{r\in[m]}\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle
, and

\displaystyle{(1.05)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\cdot(\beta^{*}_{\mathbf{u},j}m)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r^{*}}^{(t_{2}+1)},j\mathbf{u}\rangle-\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r^{*}}^{(t_{1})},j\mathbf{u}\rangle\right|

(D.90)

\displaystyle=\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\left|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{1}\leq s\leq t_{2}\\
y_{i_{s}}=j,i_{s}\notin\mathcal{W}\\
y_{i_{s}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{s}};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})>1\end{subarray}}\big(1-y_{i_{s}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{s}};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)\cdot\underbrace{\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r^{*}}^{(s)},j\mathbf{u}\rangle}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}>(\beta^{*}_{\mathbf{u},j}m)^{\frac{1}{2}}}}\right.

(D.91)

\displaystyle\qquad\left.-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{1}\leq s\leq t_{2}\\
y_{i_{s}}=j,i_{s}\notin\mathcal{W}\\
y_{i_{s}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{s}};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})<1\end{subarray}}\big(1-y_{i_{s}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{s}};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)\cdot\underbrace{\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r^{*}}^{(s)},j\mathbf{u}\rangle}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}<(1.05-\delta/4)^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot(\beta^{*}_{\mathbf{u},j}m)^{\frac{1}{2}}}}\right|,

(D.92)

where the red remarks follow from Lemma
B.5
.
Rearranging terms, we conclude from (
D.90
) that

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{1}\leq s\leq t_{2}\\
y_{i_{s}}=j,i_{s}\notin\mathcal{W}\\
y_{i_{s}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{s}};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})<1\end{subarray}}\big(1-y_{i_{s}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{s}};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)

(D.93)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\left(1.05-\delta/4\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{1}\leq s\leq t_{2}\\
y_{i_{s}}=j,i_{s}\notin\mathcal{W}\\
y_{i_{s}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{s}};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})>1\end{subarray}}\big(y_{i_{s}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{s}};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})-1\big)-\frac{m{(1.05)^{\frac{1}{2}}}}{2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}(1.05-\delta/4)^{\frac{1}{2}}},

(D.94)

Under Assumption
4.1
, with probability at least
1-1/\mathrm{poly}(d)
, it holds that

\displaystyle\left|\left\{t_{1}\leq s\leq t_{2}:y_{i_{s}}=j,i_{s}\notin\mathcal{W}\right\}\right|\geq\frac{1}{4}\cdot(t_{2}-t_{1}+1),

(D.95)

Combining (
D.93
) and (
D.95
), we can finally prove that

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s=t_{1}\\
y_{i_{s}}=j,i_{s}\notin\mathcal{W}\end{subarray}}^{t_{2}}\!\!\!\!\!\!1-y_{i_{s}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{s}};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\geq\frac{\delta}{8}\!\cdot\!\big(1-(1.05-\delta/4)^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)\!\cdot\!(t_{2}-t_{1}+1)-\frac{m{(1.05)^{\frac{1}{2}}}}{2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}(1.05-\delta/4)^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

(D.96)

Finally, for the weak data
i_{s}\in\mathcal{W}
, under Assumption
4.1
, with probability at least
1-1/\mathrm{poly}(d)
,

\displaystyle\left|\left\{t_{1}\leq s\leq t_{2}:y_{i_{s}}=j,i_{s}\in\mathcal{W}\right\}\right|\leq 2\rho\cdot(t_{2}-t_{1}+1)\leq\frac{\delta}{32}\cdot\big(1-(1.05-\delta/4)^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)\cdot(t_{2}-t_{1}+1),

(D.97)

where the second inequality follows from the condition on
\rho
by Assumption
4.1
.
By Lemma
D.2
, we have that
|1-y_{i_{s}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{s}};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})|\leq 2
for
0\leq s\leq T_{\max}^{j}
and
y_{s}=j
.
Therefore, we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s=t_{1}\\
y_{i_{s}}=j,i_{s}\in\mathcal{W}\end{subarray}}^{t_{2}}1-y_{i_{s}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{s}};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\geq-\frac{\delta}{16}\cdot\big(1-(1.05-\delta/4)^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)\cdot(t_{2}-t_{1}+1).

(D.98)

Combining (
D.96
) and (
D.98
), we can conclude that

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s=t_{1}\\
y_{i_{s}}=j\end{subarray}}^{t_{2}}1-y_{i_{s}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{s}};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\geq\frac{\delta}{16}\!\cdot\!\big(1-(1.05-\delta/4)^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)\!\cdot\!(t_{2}-t_{1}+1)-\frac{m{(1.05)^{\frac{1}{2}}}}{2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}(1.05-\delta/4)^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

(D.99)

This finishes the proof of the first part in Lemma
D.3
.
Now we consider the second part. For simplicity, we denote by
\alpha=\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}/\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}
and
\tilde{\eta}=2\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}/m
.
Consider that for any
0\leq t\leq T_{\max}^{j}
,
j\in\{\pm 1\}
, and
r\in\mathcal{V}_{j,+}^{(t)}
, due to Lemma
D.2
, the
\mathbf{v}
-sign stability condition is true on
[0,T_{\max}^{j}]
.
In view of (
D.5
), this means that

\displaystyle 1+\alpha\tilde{\eta}\cdot\big(1-y_{i_{s}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{s}};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)>0,\quad\forall 0\leq s\leq T_{\max}^{j}\,\,\,\text{s.t.}\,\,\,y_{i_{s}}=j.

(D.100)

Then for any
t_{1}\leq t\leq T_{\max}^{j}
,
t_{1}\leq s\leq t
, and
r\in\mathcal{C}_{j,+}^{(t)}
, since
-2\leq 1-y_{i_{s}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{s}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\leq 2
due to Lemma
D.2
, we can lower bound the relative increment as

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s=t_{1}\\
y_{i_{s}}=j\end{subarray}}^{t}

\displaystyle\log\Big(1+\alpha\tilde{\eta}\big(1-y_{i_{s}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{s}};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)\Big)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s=t_{1}\\
y_{i_{s}}=j\end{subarray}}^{t}\int_{0}^{\alpha}\frac{\tilde{\eta}\big(1-y_{i_{s}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{s}};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)}{1+\tilde{\eta}z\big(1-y_{i_{s}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{s}};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)}dz

(D.101)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s=t_{1}\\
y_{i_{s}}=j\end{subarray}}^{t}\int_{0}^{\alpha}\frac{\tilde{\eta}\Big(\big(1-y_{i_{s}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{s}};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)+2\Big)}{1+\tilde{\eta}z\big(1-y_{i_{s}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{s}};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)}dz-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s=t_{1}\\
y_{i_{s}}=j\end{subarray}}^{t}\int_{0}^{\alpha}\frac{2\tilde{\eta}}{1+\tilde{\eta}z\big(1-y_{i_{s}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{s}};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)}dz

(D.102)

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s=t_{1}\\
y_{i_{s}}=j\end{subarray}}^{t}\int_{0}^{\alpha}\frac{\tilde{\eta}\Big(\big(1-y_{i_{s}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{s}};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)+2\Big)}{1+2\tilde{\eta}z}dz-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s=t_{1}\\
y_{i_{s}}=j\end{subarray}}^{t}\int_{0}^{\alpha}\frac{2\tilde{\eta}}{1-2\tilde{\eta}z}dz

(D.103)

\displaystyle\geq\int_{0}^{\alpha}\frac{\tilde{\eta}\Big(\sum_{s=t_{1},y_{i_{s}}=j}^{t}\big(1-y_{i_{s}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{s}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\big)+2N_{j}(t_{0},t)\Big)}{1+2\tilde{\eta}z}dz-\int_{0}^{\alpha}\frac{2\tilde{\eta}N_{j}(t_{1},t)}{1-2\tilde{\eta}z}dz,

(D.104)

where for simplicity we denote
N_{j}(t_{1},t)=|\{t_{1}\leq s\leq t:y_{i_{s}}=j\}|
.
Now we can use Proposition
D.3
to lower bound the right hand side of (
D.104
),
Denoting
\epsilon=\delta\cdot(1-(1.05-\delta/4)^{\frac{1}{2}})/16
, we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s=t_{1}\\
y_{i_{s}}=j\end{subarray}}^{t}

\displaystyle\log\Big\{1+\alpha\tilde{\eta}\big(1-y_{i_{s}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{s}};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)\Big\}

(D.105)

\displaystyle\geq\int_{0}^{\alpha}\frac{\tilde{\eta}\Big(\epsilon(t-t_{1}+1)-\Delta+2N_{j}(t_{1},t)\Big)}{1+2\tilde{\eta}z}dz-\int_{0}^{\alpha}\frac{2\tilde{\eta}N_{j}(t_{1},t)}{1-2\tilde{\eta}}dz

(D.106)

\displaystyle\geq\bigg(\frac{\epsilon}{2}(t-t_{1}+1)-\frac{\Delta}{2}+N_{j}(t_{1},t)\bigg)\cdot\log(1+2\alpha\tilde{\eta})+N_{j}(t_{1},t)\cdot\log(1-2\alpha\tilde{\eta})

(D.107)

\displaystyle\geq\bigg(\frac{\epsilon}{2}(t-t_{1}+1)-\frac{\Delta}{2}\bigg)\cdot\log(1+2\alpha\tilde{\eta})+N_{j}(t_{1},t)\cdot\log\big\{(1+2\alpha\tilde{\eta})\cdot(1-2\alpha\tilde{\eta})\},

(D.108)

where
\Delta
is defined in Lemma
D.3
.
Moreover since for our choice of
\alpha\tilde{\eta}\ll 1
in Assumption
4.1
,

\displaystyle\log(1+2\alpha\tilde{\eta})\geq\alpha\tilde{\eta},\quad\log\big\{(1+2\alpha\tilde{\eta})\cdot(1-2\alpha\tilde{\eta})\big\}=\log\big\{1-4\alpha^{2}\tilde{\eta}^{2}\big\}\geq-2\alpha^{2}\tilde{\eta}^{2},

(D.109)

and using the fact that with probability at least
1-1/\mathrm{poly}(d)
it holds that
N_{j}(t_{1},t)\leq(t-t_{1}+1)/2
,
we finally have that

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s=t_{1}\\
y_{i_{s}}=j\end{subarray}}^{t}\log\Big\{1+\alpha\tilde{\eta}\big(1-y_{i_{s}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{s}};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)\Big\}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\alpha\tilde{\eta}}{2}\!\cdot\!(\epsilon-2\alpha\tilde{\eta})\!\cdot\!(t_{1}-t_{1}+1)-\Delta\cdot\log(1+2\alpha\tilde{\eta})

(D.110)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{4}\alpha\tilde{\eta}\cdot\epsilon\cdot(t_{1}-t_{1}+1)-2\alpha\tilde{\eta}\cdot\Delta.

(D.111)

Finally, using (
D.5
) again, we can lower bound our target as

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t+1)}},{j\mathbf{v}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{1})}},{j\mathbf{v}}\rangle\cdot\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}s=t_{1}\\
y_{i_{s}}=j\end{subarray}}^{t}\Big(1+\alpha\tilde{\eta}\big(1-y_{i_{s}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{s}};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)\Big)

(D.112)

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{1})}},{j\mathbf{v}}\rangle\cdot\exp\left\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s=t_{1}\\
y_{i_{s}}=j\end{subarray}}^{t}\log\Big\{1+\alpha\tilde{\eta}\big(1-y_{i_{s}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{s}};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)\Big\}\right\}

(D.113)

\displaystyle\geq\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{1})}},{j\mathbf{v}}\rangle\cdot\exp\left\{\frac{1}{4}\alpha\tilde{\eta}\cdot\epsilon\cdot(t_{1}-t_{1}+1)-2\alpha\tilde{\eta}\cdot\Delta\right\}.

(D.114)

Plugging in the definition of
\epsilon
,
\Delta
,
\alpha
, and
\tilde{\eta}
, we can arrive at

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t+1)}},{j\mathbf{v}}\rangle\geq\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{1})}},{j\mathbf{v}}\rangle\cdot\exp\left\{\frac{\eta\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}}{32m}\!\cdot\!\big(\delta-\delta(1.05-\delta/4)^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)\!\cdot\!(t-t_{1}+1)-\frac{\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}{(1.05)^{\frac{1}{2}}}}{\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}(1.05-\delta/4)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right\}.

(D.115)

This finishes the proof of Lemma
D.3
.
∎

D.6
Proof of Technical Results

D.6.1
Proof of Proposition
D.1

Proof of Proposition
D.1
.

From Equation (
D.4
), for
r\in\mathcal{U}_{j,+}
and
t^{\prime}\in(t_{j}(s),t_{j}(s+1)]
, we can infer that

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t^{\prime})}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(s))}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle+\sum_{t=t_{j}(s)}^{t^{\prime}-1}\frac{\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\big(1-\tilde{f}_{t}\big)\cdot\sigma^{\prime}\bigl(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\cdot jy_{i_{t}}\bigr)\cdot\mathbf{1}\{i_{t}\in\mathcal{W}\}.

(D.116)

The definition of
t_{j}(s)
implies that for
t\in(t_{j}(s),t_{j}(s+1))
,
y_{i_{t}}j=-1
. On the other hand
y_{i_{t_{j}(s)}}j=1
, hence we obtain that

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t^{\prime})}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(s))}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle+\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\big(1-\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(s)}\big)\cdot\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\cdot\mathbf{1}\{i_{t_{j}(s)}\in\mathcal{W}\}.

(D.117)

Since the sign stability holds, this multiplication by a non-negative factor does not change the sign of the inner products. Therefore we have that

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(s+1))}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(s+1)-1)}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle=\cdots=\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(s)+1)}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle

(D.118)

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(s))}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\cdot\big(1+\tilde{\eta}\cdot(1-\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(s)})\big).

(D.119)

which concludes our result for
\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle,\;r\in\mathcal{U}_{j,+}
. Other result can be proved analogously and are omitted here.
∎

D.6.2
Proof of Lemma
B.5
for Multiple Data Setting

Proof of Lemma
B.5
(multiple data setting).

In the multiple data setting, the (positive) inner products only changes at the steps where the corresponding data label aligns with the directions of the neurons (i.e.,
j=\pm 1
). Define

\displaystyle\beta^{*,(t_{1})}_{\mathbf{u},j}

\displaystyle=\frac{\max_{r\in[m]}\sigma(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{1})}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle)}{\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{1})}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle)}.

(D.120)

Again, the local sign stability assumption ensures that each inner product grows proportionally and the superscript
(t)
in the neuron index sets can be dropped, with

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in\mathcal{U}_{j,+}}\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{1})}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\big)\cdot\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}t^{\prime}\in[t_{1},t-1]:\\
y_{i_{t}}=j,\;i_{t}\notin\mathcal{W}\end{subarray}}\left(1+\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\big(1-y_{i_{t^{\prime}}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t^{\prime}}};\mathbf{W}^{(t^{\prime})})\big)\right)^{2}

(D.121)

\displaystyle=\frac{\max_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{1})}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\big)}{m\beta^{*,(t_{1})}_{\mathbf{u},j}}\cdot\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}t^{\prime}\in[t_{1},t-1]:\\
y_{i_{t^{\prime}}}=j,\;i_{t}\notin\mathcal{W}\end{subarray}}\left(1+\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\big(1-y_{i_{t^{\prime}}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t^{\prime}}};\mathbf{W}^{(t^{\prime})})\big)\right)^{2}

(D.122)

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma\big(\max_{r\in[m]}\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\big)}{m\beta^{*,(t_{1})}_{\mathbf{u},j}}

(D.123)

Here the first and the third equality is true because Equation (
B.2
) implies that all the positive
\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle
iterates by sequentially multiplying the same factor

\displaystyle 1+\frac{2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\big(1-yf(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t^{\prime})})\big).

(D.124)

The second equality comes from the definition of
\beta^{*,(t_{1})}_{\mathbf{u}}
in Lemma
B.5
.

Therefore,
m^{-1}\cdot\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle)>c
implies that
\sigma(\max_{r\in[m]}\langle{\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)}},{y\mathbf{u}}\rangle)>\beta^{*,(t_{1})}_{\mathbf{u},j}mc
and the desired lower bound follows. The upper bound can be proved analogously.
∎

D.6.3
Proof of Proposition
D.4

Proof of Proposition
D.4
.

We prove the result by induction.
For the step
t=t_{1}
, the result holds trivially.
Suppose that this result holds for each step
t_{1},\cdots,t
, then
by (
D.7
), we have that

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t+1)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{1})},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle+\sum_{s=t_{1}}^{t}\frac{\eta\cdot jy_{i_{s}}}{m}\cdot\big(1-y_{i_{s}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{s}};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)\cdot\left(\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(s)}\cdot\bm{\xi}_{i_{s}}\rangle)\cdot\langle\bm{\xi}_{i_{s}},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\right.

(D.125)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left.\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(s)}\cdot\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i_{s}}\rangle)\cdot\langle\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i_{s}},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\cdot\mathbf{1}\{i_{s}\in\mathcal{W}\}\right)

(D.126)

\displaystyle=\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{1})},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s=t_{1}\\
i_{s}=i\end{subarray}}^{t}\frac{\eta\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot jy_{i_{s}}}{m}\cdot\big(1-y_{i}f(\mathbf{x}_{i};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(s)}\cdot\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle)

(D.127)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s=t_{1}\\
i_{s}\neq i\end{subarray}}^{t}\frac{\eta\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot jy_{i_{s}}}{m}\cdot\big(1-y_{i_{s}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{s}};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)\cdot\left(\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(s)}\cdot\bm{\xi}_{i_{s}}\rangle)\cdot\frac{\langle\bm{\xi}_{i_{s}},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle}{\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}}\right.

(D.128)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\left.\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(s)}\cdot\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i_{s}}\rangle)\cdot\frac{\langle\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i_{s}},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle}{\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}}\cdot\mathbf{1}\{i_{s}\in\mathcal{W}\}\right)

(D.129)

Taking absolute value, we have that

\displaystyle\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t+1)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{1})},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\right|+\left|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s=t_{1}\\
i_{s}=i\end{subarray}}^{t}\frac{\eta\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot jy_{i_{s}}}{m}\cdot\big(1-y_{i}f(\mathbf{x}_{i};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(s)}\cdot\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle)\right|

(D.130)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s=t_{1}\\
i_{s}\neq i\end{subarray}}^{t}\frac{\eta\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot jy_{i_{s}}}{m}\cdot\big(1-y_{i_{s}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{s}};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big)\right.

(D.131)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\left.\cdot\left(\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(s)}\cdot\bm{\xi}_{i_{s}}\rangle)\cdot\frac{\langle\bm{\xi}_{i_{s}},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle}{\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}}+\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(s)}\cdot\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i_{s}}\rangle)\cdot\frac{\langle\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i_{s}},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle}{\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}}\cdot\mathbf{1}\{i_{s}\in\mathcal{W}\}\right)\right|

(D.132)

Applying the definition of
\Upsilon^{(s)}
, we further obtain that

\displaystyle\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t+1)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Upsilon^{(t_{1})}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s=t_{1}\\
i_{s}=i\end{subarray}}^{t}\frac{2\eta\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\big|1-y_{i}f(\mathbf{x}_{i};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big|\cdot\Upsilon^{(s)}

(D.133)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s=t_{1}\\
i_{s}\neq i\end{subarray}}^{t}\frac{2\eta\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\big|1-y_{i_{s}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{s}};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\big|\cdot\Upsilon^{(s)}\cdot\left(\frac{|\langle\bm{\xi}_{i_{s}},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle|}{\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}}+\frac{|\langle\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i_{s}},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle|}{\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}}\right).

(D.134)

Now using Lemma
D.2
, for
s\leq\min_{j\in\{\pm 1\}}\{T_{\max}^{j}\}
, we have that
|1-y_{i_{s}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{s}};\mathbf{W}^{(s)})|\leq 2
.
Also, by Lemma
A.3
, it holds that
\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 3\sigma_{p}^{2}d/2
and
|\langle\bm{\xi},\bm{\xi}^{\prime}\rangle|/\|\bm{\xi}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(d^{-1/2})
.
Thus we can further upper bound (
D.134
) as

\displaystyle\max_{i\in[n]}\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t+1)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Upsilon^{(t_{1})}+\frac{6\eta\sigma_{p}^{2}d}{m}\cdot\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s=t_{1}\\
i_{s}=i\end{subarray}}^{t}\Upsilon^{(s)}+\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(d^{-1/2})\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s=t_{1}\\
i_{s}\neq i\end{subarray}}^{t}\Upsilon^{(s)}\right)

(D.135)

\displaystyle=\Upsilon^{(t_{1})}+\frac{6\eta\sigma_{p}^{2}d}{m}\cdot\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s=t_{1}\\
i_{s}=i\end{subarray}}^{t}\Upsilon^{(t_{1})}+\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(d^{-1/2})\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s=t_{1}\\
i_{s}\neq i\end{subarray}}^{t}\Upsilon^{(t_{1})}\right)

(D.136)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{6\eta\sigma_{p}^{2}d}{m}\cdot\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s=t_{1}\\
i_{s}=i\end{subarray}}^{t}\big(\Upsilon^{(t_{1})}-\Upsilon^{(s)}\big)+\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(d^{-1/2})\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s=t_{1}\\
i_{s}\neq i\end{subarray}}^{t}\big(\Upsilon^{(t_{1})}-\Upsilon^{(s)}\big)\right).

(D.137)

By our induction, we have that
\Upsilon^{(s)}-\Upsilon^{(t_{1})}\leq\Upsilon^{(t_{1})}\cdot\epsilon
, for which we can further bound (
D.135
) as

\displaystyle\max_{i\in[n]}\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t+1)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Upsilon^{(t_{1})}\cdot\left[1+\frac{6\eta\sigma_{p}^{2}d}{m}\cdot(1+\epsilon)\cdot\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s=t_{1}\\
i_{s}=i\end{subarray}}^{t}1+\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(d^{-1/2})\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s=t_{1}\\
i_{s}\neq i\end{subarray}}^{t}1\right)\right]

(D.138)

\displaystyle\leq\Upsilon^{(t_{1})}\cdot\left[1+\frac{6\eta\sigma_{p}^{2}d}{m}\cdot(1+\epsilon)\cdot\left(\frac{2(t-t_{1}+1)}{n}+\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(d^{-1/2})\cdot(t-t_{1}+1)\right)\right]

(D.139)

\displaystyle\leq\Upsilon^{(t_{1})}\cdot\left[1+\frac{18\eta\sigma_{p}^{2}d}{mn}\cdot(1+\epsilon)\cdot(t-t_{1}+1)\right],

(D.140)

where in the first inequality we have utilized the fact that
|\{t_{1}\leq s\leq t:i_{s}=i\}|\leq 2(t-t_{1}+1)/n
, and in the last inequality we apply the condition that
d=\widetilde{\Omega}(n^{2})
by Assumption
4.1
.
Therefore, when
t\leq t_{1}+\widetilde{T}_{(\bm{\xi})}-1
with
\widetilde{T}_{(\bm{\xi})}=\widetilde{\Theta}(mn\cdot\eta^{-1}\cdot\epsilon\cdot(1+\epsilon)^{-1}\cdot(\sigma_{p}^{2}d)^{-1})
, it holds that

\displaystyle\max_{i\in[n]}\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t+1)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Upsilon^{(t_{1})}\cdot(1+\epsilon).

(D.141)

By using the same argument as proving (
D.141
), we can also show that for
t\leq t_{1}+\widetilde{T}_{(\bm{\xi})}-1

\displaystyle\max_{i\in\mathcal{W}}\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t+1)},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|\leq\Upsilon^{(t_{1})}\cdot(1+\epsilon).

(D.142)

By combining (
D.141
) and (
D.142
), we can arrive at

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{(t+1)}=\max_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\left\{\max_{i\in[n]}\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t+1)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\right|,\max_{i\in\mathcal{W}}\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t+1)},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|\right\}\leq\Upsilon^{(t_{1})}\cdot(1+\epsilon).

(D.143)

Thus we have proved our induction statement for step
t+1
.
Repeating the induction completes the proof of Proposition
D.4
.
∎

D.6.4
Proof of Proposition
D.5

Proof of Proposition
D.5
.

We expand
\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k})}
as follows.

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}-1))}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\big)\cdot\left(1+\tilde{\eta}\big(1-\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}-1)}\big)\right)^{2}

(D.144)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}-1))}},{j\mathbf{v}}\rangle\big)\cdot\left(1+\alpha\tilde{\eta}\big(1-\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}-1)}\big)\right)^{2}

(D.145)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(-\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}-1))}},{-j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\big)\cdot\left(1-\tilde{\eta}\big(1-\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}-1)}\big)\right)^{2}

(D.146)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(-\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}-1))}},{-j\mathbf{v}}\rangle\big)\cdot\left(1-\alpha\tilde{\eta}\big(1-\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}-1)}\big)\right)^{2}

(D.147)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left|\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}))}},{\bm{\xi}_{i_{t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k})}}}\rangle\big)-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}))}},{\bm{\xi}_{i_{t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k})}}}\rangle\big)\right|

(D.148)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\left(\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}-1))}},{j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\big)+\sigma\big(\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}-1))}},{j\mathbf{v}}\rangle\big)\right.

(D.149)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\left.-\sigma\big(-\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}-1))}},{-j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\big)-\sigma\big(-\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}-1))}},{-j\mathbf{v}}\rangle\big)\right)\cdot\left(1+\tilde{\eta}\big(1-\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}-1)}\big)\right)^{2}

(D.150)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\left(\sigma\big(-\langle{\mathbf{w}_{-j,r}^{(t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}-1))}},{-j\mathbf{u}}\rangle\big)+\sigma\big(-\langle{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}-1))}},{-j\mathbf{v}}\rangle\big)\right)\cdot\left(2\tilde{\eta}\big(1-\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}-1)}\big)\right)

(D.151)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+{\Upsilon^{(t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}))}}^{2}

(D.152)

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}-1)}\cdot\left(1+\tilde{\eta}\big(1-\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}-1)}\big)\right)^{2}+2E_{t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}-1)}+{\Upsilon^{(t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}))}}^{2}.

(D.153)

By Assumption
B.2
we know that
\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k})}>1+\delta
.
From the discussion in the proof of Lemma
B.9
, we know that once

\displaystyle 2E_{t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}-1)}+{\Upsilon^{(t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}))}}^{2}\leq\delta,

(D.154)

we have that

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}-1)}\geq\frac{\tilde{\eta}+2-\sqrt{\tilde{\eta}^{2}+4\tilde{\eta}}}{2\tilde{\eta}},

(D.155)

and that

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k})}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\tilde{\eta}(1-\tilde{f}_{t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}-1)})\right)^{2}+E_{t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}-1)}+{\Upsilon^{(t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}))}}^{2}

(D.156)

\displaystyle\leq\bigl(\tilde{\eta}/2+\sqrt{\tilde{\eta}^{2}/4+\tilde{\eta}}\bigr)^{2}+E_{t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}-1)}+{\Upsilon^{(t_{j}(\bar{S}_{j,k}))}}^{2}.

(D.157)

This finishes the proof of Proposition
D.5

∎

Appendix E
Multiple Training Data Case: Small Learning Rate Regime

This section focuses on the multiple training data setup with a small learning rate.

Recall that
\mathcal{W}
is the index set of training data points which lack the strong feature patch.
By (
2.5
), the CNN weights are updated according to

\displaystyle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t+1)}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)}-\frac{j\eta}{m}\cdot\big(f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})-y_{i_{t}}\big)\cdot\left(\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y_{i_{t}}\mathbf{u}\rangle)\cdot y_{i_{t}}\mathbf{u}\cdot\mathbf{1}\{i_{t}\notin\mathcal{W}\}\right.

(E.1)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y_{i_{t}}\mathbf{v}\rangle)\cdot y_{i_{t}}\mathbf{v}+\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)}\cdot\bm{\xi}_{i_{t}}\rangle)\cdot\bm{\xi}_{i_{t}}

(E.2)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left.\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)}\cdot\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i_{t}}\rangle)\cdot\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i_{t}},\cdot\mathbf{1}\{i_{t}\in\mathcal{W}\}\right).

(E.3)

Subsequently, by (
E.3
) the update formulas of those inner products of interests are given by

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t+1)},j\mathbf{u}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},j\mathbf{u}\rangle+\frac{\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\big(1-y_{i_{t}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\big)\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},j\mathbf{u}\rangle jy_{i_{t}})\cdot\mathbf{1}\{i_{t}\notin\mathcal{W}\},

(E.4)

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t+1)},j\mathbf{v}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},j\mathbf{v}\rangle+\frac{\eta\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\big(1-y_{i_{t}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\big)\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)}\cdot j\mathbf{v}\rangle jy_{i_{t}}),

(E.5)

and

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t+1)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle+\frac{\eta\cdot jy_{i_{t}}}{m}\cdot\big(1-y_{i_{t}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\big)\cdot\left(\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)}\cdot\bm{\xi}_{i_{t}}\rangle)\cdot\langle\bm{\xi}_{i_{t}},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\right.

(E.6)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left.\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)}\cdot\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i_{t}}\rangle)\cdot\langle\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i_{t}},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\cdot\mathbf{1}\{i_{t}\in\mathcal{W}\}\right).

(E.7)

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t+1)},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\frac{\eta\cdot jy_{i_{t}}}{m}\cdot\big(1-y_{i_{t}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\big)\cdot\left(\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i_{t}}\rangle)\cdot\langle\bm{\xi}_{i_{t}},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right.

(E.8)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left.\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i_{t}}\rangle)\cdot\langle\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i_{t}},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\cdot\mathbf{1}\{i_{t}\in\mathcal{W}\}\right),\quad i\in\mathcal{W}.

(E.9)

For convenience, we also write
\ell^{(t)}_{i}=f(\mathbf{x}_{i};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})-y_{i}
as the fitting residual.

The result of this section relies on the following conditions on the data model and the initialization.

Assumption E.1

(Conditions on hyperparameters)
.

Suppose that the following holds.
For some
\epsilon\in(0,1),

1.

The weight initialization scale
\sigma_{0}=\widetilde{\Theta}(\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{-1})
;

2.

Strong signal strength
\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}>\widetilde{\Omega}(m/\sqrt{n\epsilon})\cdot\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d}
and weak signal strength
\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d}\geq\widetilde{\Omega}\left(m/\sqrt{n\epsilon}\right)\cdot\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}
;

3.

The dimension
d
satisfies
d=\Omega(\mathrm{polylog}(m,n))
.

Theorem E.2

(Restatement of Proposition
4.5
)
.

Under Assumption
E.1
, choosing the learning rate
\eta\leq m/6\|\mathbf{u}\|^{2}_{2}
small enough and
\epsilon^{\prime}\in(0,1)
, then with high probability
1-1/\mathrm{poly}(d)
, there exist

\displaystyle T^{\dagger}=\frac{4m}{\eta(1-\tau)(1-\rho)\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{2\iota}{\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}}\right),\quad T=T^{\dagger}+\left\lfloor\frac{Cm^{3}}{2\eta\epsilon\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}\right\rfloor,\quad T^{\prime}=T+\left\lfloor\frac{\left\|\mathbf{W}^{(T)}-\mathbf{W}^{\star}\right\|_{F}^{2}}{2\eta\epsilon}\right\rfloor,

with
\tau
,
\iota
defined in (
E.15
), (
E.16
) such that: (i) the average loss on samples
\mathcal{W}^{c}
decreases to
3\epsilon
over iterations
[T^{\dagger},T]
, i.e.

\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{W}^{c}}\min_{T^{\dagger}\leq t\leq T}\left\{y_{i}-f(\mathbf{x}_{i};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\right\}^{2}\leq 3\epsilon,

(ii) average loss on samples
\mathcal{W}
decreases to
3\epsilon^{\prime}
over iterations
[T,T^{\prime}]
, i.e.

\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{W}}\min_{T\leq t\leq T^{\prime}}\left(y_{i}-f(\mathbf{x}_{i};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\right)^{2}\leq 3\epsilon^{\prime},

(iii) the model does not learn weak signal
\mathbf{v}
well enough even until
T^{\prime}
, compared to initialization, i.e.

\displaystyle\max_{j,r}\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle\right|\leq 2\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2},\quad\forall t\leq T^{\prime}.

(E.10)

In the multiple training data small learning rate regime, the dynamics go through three stages, which we characterize in Appendices
E.1
,
E.2
, and
E.3
, respectively.
The following lemma plays an important role in the exponentially increasing stage (stage 1), for which we single it out here.

Lemma E.3

(Derivative lower bound)
.

For any
0<\tau<1
to be tuned later, suppose at some time
t
there holds

\max_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\left\{\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}\rangle\right|,\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle\right|,\max_{i\in[n]}\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\right|,\max_{i\in\mathcal{W}}\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|\right\}\leq\sqrt{\frac{\tau}{3}},

then we can lower bound the fitting residual
-y\ell^{(t)}_{i}\geq 1-\tau
for every
i\in[n]
.

Proof of Lemma
E.3
.

Plug into the CNN model definition (
2.3
), we have that

\displaystyle-y\ell^{(t)}_{i}=1-F_{y}(\mathbf{x}_{i};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})+F_{-y}(\mathbf{x}_{i};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\geq 1-F_{y}(\mathbf{x}_{i};\mathbf{W}^{(t)}).

If
i\in\mathcal{W}^{c}
, we can upper bound
F_{y}(\mathbf{x}_{i};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})
further by

\displaystyle F_{y}(\mathbf{x}_{i};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)},y\mathbf{u}\rangle)+\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)},y\mathbf{v}\rangle)+\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{r\in[m]}\left\{\langle\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)},y\mathbf{u}\rangle^{2}+\langle\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)},y\mathbf{v}\rangle^{2}+\langle\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle^{2}\right\}\leq\tau.

Otherwise, if
i\in\mathcal{W}
, we also have

\displaystyle F_{y}(\mathbf{x}_{i};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}\rangle)+\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)},y\mathbf{v}\rangle)+\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{r\in[m]}\left\{\langle\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}\rangle^{2}+\langle\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)},y\mathbf{v}\rangle^{2}+\langle\mathbf{w}_{y,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle^{2}\right\}\leq\tau.

Then it follows that
-y\ell^{(t)}\geq 1-\tau
.
∎

E.1
Stage 1. Learning Strong Signal Exponentially Fast

In this stage, we mainly track the maximal inner product between
\mathbf{w}
and the signal vectors
\mathbf{v}
and
\mathbf{u}
, with extra attention to the maximal inner product between
\mathbf{w}
and the noise vectors.

\displaystyle\Psi^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\max_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle\right|,\quad\Phi^{(t)}=\max_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}\rangle\right|,

\displaystyle\Gamma^{(t)}_{i}

\displaystyle=\max_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\right|,\quad i\in[n],

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Gamma}^{(t)}_{i}

\displaystyle=\max_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|,\quad i\in\mathcal{W}.

Lemma E.4

(First stage: noise)
.

Under the same conditions as Theorem
E.2
, ever since initialization, at least until time

\displaystyle T_{+}:=\frac{nm}{3\eta(4+\rho)\sigma_{p}^{2}d},

(E.11)

there still holds that

\max_{i\in[n]}\Gamma_{i}^{(t)}\leq\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d},\quad\max_{i\in\mathcal{W}}\widetilde{\Gamma}_{i}^{(t)}\leq\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d}.

(E.12)

Proof of Lemma
E.4
.

For those inner products with noise vectors,
\forall i\in[n]
, the updating rules become

\displaystyle\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t+1)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\right|+\frac{\eta}{m}\cdot\left|\ell^{(t)}_{i_{t}}\right|\cdot\left(\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)}\cdot\bm{\xi}_{i_{t}}\rangle)\cdot\left|\langle\bm{\xi}_{i_{t}},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\right|+\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)}\cdot\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i_{t}}\rangle)\cdot\left|\langle\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i_{t}},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\right|\cdot\mathbf{1}\{i_{t}\in\mathcal{W}\}\right)

(E.13)

\displaystyle\leq\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\right|+\frac{6\eta}{m}\cdot\left(\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i_{t}}\rangle\right|\cdot\left|\langle\bm{\xi}_{i_{t}},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\right|+\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i_{t}}\rangle\right|\cdot\left|\langle\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i_{t}},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\right|\cdot\mathbf{1}\{i_{t}\in\mathcal{W}\}\right).

(E.14)

By taking maximum over
r\in[m]
, we conclude that

\Gamma^{(t+1)}_{i}\leq\Gamma^{(t)}_{i}+\frac{6\eta}{m}\cdot\left(\Gamma_{i_{t}}^{(t)}\cdot\left|\langle\bm{\xi}_{i_{t}},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\right|+\widetilde{\Gamma}_{i_{t}}^{(t)}\cdot\left|\langle\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i_{t}},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\right|\cdot\mathbf{1}\{i_{t}\in\mathcal{W}\}\right),\quad\forall i\in[n].

Similarly, we also have that

\widetilde{\Gamma}^{(t+1)}_{i}\leq\widetilde{\Gamma}^{(t)}_{i}+\frac{6\eta}{m}\cdot\left(\Gamma_{i_{t}}^{(t)}\cdot\left|\langle\bm{\xi}_{i_{t}},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|+\widetilde{\Gamma}_{i_{t}}^{(t)}\cdot\left|\langle\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i_{t}},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|\cdot\mathbf{1}\{i_{t}\in\mathcal{W}\}\right),\quad\forall i\in\mathcal{W}.

We then use induction to rigorously prove our conclusions.
Firstly, (
E.12
) holds at time
t=0
.
Now suppose that (
E.12
) holds until some
\widetilde{T}<T_{+}
. Fixing some
i\in[n]
,

\displaystyle\Gamma^{(\widetilde{T}+1)}_{i}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{6\eta\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d}}{m}\cdot\sum_{t=0}^{\widetilde{T}}\left(\left|\langle\bm{\xi}_{i_{t}},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\right|+\left|\langle\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i_{t}},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\right|\cdot\mathbf{1}\{i_{t}\in\mathcal{W}\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{6\eta\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d}}{m}\cdot\left(\frac{3\widetilde{T}\sigma_{p}^{2}d}{2n}+2\widetilde{T}\cdot(1+\rho)\cdot\sigma_{p}^{2}\sqrt{d\log(4n^{2}/p)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3\eta\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d}(4+\rho)\widetilde{T}\sigma_{p}^{2}d}{nm}

\displaystyle\leq\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d}.

The first inequality is by induction hypothesis. The second inequality is because that there are at most
\widetilde{T}/n
many
i_{t}
’s would equal
i
and at most
\rho\widetilde{T}
many
i_{t}
’s would be in
\mathcal{W}
, and we also use Lemma
A.3
to control the correlations between noise vectors. The third inequality is by
d\geq 16n^{2}\log(4n^{2}/p)
for Assumption
E.1
, while the last inequality is due to
\widetilde{T}<T_{+}
.
Similarly, we can also control
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{i}^{(t+1)}
for some fixed
i\in\mathcal{W}
as

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Gamma}^{(\widetilde{T}+1)}_{i}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{6\eta\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d}}{m}\cdot\sum_{t=0}^{\widetilde{T}}\left(\left|\langle\bm{\xi}_{i_{t}},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|+\left|\langle\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i_{t}},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|\cdot\mathbf{1}\{i_{t}\in\mathcal{W}\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{6\eta\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d}}{m}\left(\frac{3\widetilde{T}\sigma_{p}^{2}d}{2n}+2\widetilde{T}\cdot(1+\rho)\cdot\sigma_{p}^{2}\sqrt{d\log(4n^{2}/p)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d},

where the second inequality is because there are at most
\widetilde{T}/n
many
i_{t}
’s would equal
i\in\mathcal{W}
. In conclusion, (
E.12
) holds at least until
T_{+}
.
∎

In the following, we would take

\displaystyle\tau

\displaystyle=\max\left\{2\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}\left(2\log(16m/p)\right)^{1/2-\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}/4(\sigma_{p}^{2}d)},1-\frac{6\sqrt{2}\sigma_{p}^{2}d}{\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}\log(2/\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)})}\right\},

(E.15)

\displaystyle\iota

\displaystyle=2\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}\cdot\exp\left\{\frac{(1-\tau)(1-\rho)\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}{3(4+\rho)\sigma_{p}^{2}d}\right\}.

(E.16)

By the conditions in Assumption
E.1
on
\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}/\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}
, we find
\tau,\iota
both constant in
(0,1)
.

Lemma E.5

(First stage: signal)
.

Under the same conditions as Theorem
E.2
, there exists time

T^{\dagger}=\frac{4m}{\eta(1-\tau)(1-\rho)\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{2\iota}{\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}}\right),

such that: (i) the model learns strong signal to a constant level,

\max_{r\in[m]}\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(T^{\dagger})},j\mathbf{u}\rangle\geq\iota,\quad\forall j\in\{\pm 1\},

(ii) compared to the random initialization, the model does not learn weak signal that much, i.e.,

\displaystyle\max_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(T^{\dagger})},\mathbf{v}\rangle\right|\leq 2\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}

(E.17)

Proof of Lemma
E.5
.

Firstly, we would find
\{\Psi^{(t)},\Phi^{(t)}\}_{t\geq 0}
having an exponentially growing upper bound. Recursively, we would have that

\displaystyle\Psi^{(t+1)}

\displaystyle\leq\Psi^{(t)}+\max_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\left|\frac{jy\eta}{m}\cdot\big(f(\mathbf{x}_{i};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})-y\big)\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y\mathbf{v}\rangle)\cdot\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}\right|

(E.18)

\displaystyle=\Psi^{(t)}+\frac{\eta}{m}\cdot\left|\ell^{(t)}_{i}\right|\cdot\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\max_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y\mathbf{v}\rangle)

(E.19)

\displaystyle\leq\Psi^{(t)}+\frac{2\eta}{m}\cdot\left|\ell^{(t)}_{i}\right|\cdot\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\Psi^{(t)}

(E.20)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(\frac{6\eta\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\right)\cdot\Psi^{(t)}.

(E.21)

Therefore, we have that

\displaystyle\Psi^{(t)}\leq\exp\left(\frac{6\eta\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}t}{m}\right)\cdot\Psi^{(0)}\leq\exp\left(\frac{6\eta\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}t}{m}\right)\cdot\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}

(E.22)

It follows similarly that

\displaystyle\Phi^{(t)}\leq\exp\left(\frac{6\eta(1-\rho)\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}t}{m}\right)\cdot\Phi^{(0)}\leq\exp\left(\frac{6\eta(1-\rho)\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}t}{m}\right)\cdot\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}.

(E.23)

The extra factor
1-\rho
appears because only a
1-\rho
proportion of data points would contain
\mathbf{u}
, and therefore contribute to evolution of
\Phi^{(t)}
. Note that growing rates of these two bounds differ a lot due to the different magnitudes of
(1-\rho)\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}
and
\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}
.

Our subsequent analysis illustrates that
\Phi^{(t)}
can grow into a constant-level magnitude since strong signal
\mathbf{u}
is significant enough. We can track how well our model learns
\mathbf{u}
by

A^{(t)}_{1}=\max_{r\in[m],i_{t}\notin\mathcal{W}}\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}\rangle,\quad A^{(t)}_{-1}=\max_{r\in[m],i_{t}\notin\mathcal{W}}\langle\mathbf{w}_{-1,r}^{(t)},-\mathbf{u}\rangle.

By definition,
A^{(t)}_{1},A^{(t)}_{-1}\leq\Phi^{(t)}
also admits an exponentially upper bound in (
E.23
) .
For a certain
\tau\in(0,1)
, due to the exponential upper bounds (
E.22
) and (
E.23
),
\max\{\Phi^{(t)},\Psi^{(t)}\}\leq\sqrt{\tau/3}
is true at least until

T_{1}=\frac{m}{6\eta(1-\rho)\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{\sqrt{\tau/2}}{\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}}\right).

Moreover, since we have
(1-\rho)\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}\gg\sigma_{p}^{2}d/n
by Assumption
E.1
, we also know
T_{1}\leq T_{+}
where
T_{+}
comes from Lemma
E.4
.
Therefore

\displaystyle\Gamma_{i}^{(t)}\leq\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d}\leq\sqrt{\tau/3},\quad\widetilde{\Gamma}_{i}^{(t)}\leq\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d}\leq\sqrt{\tau/3},\quad\forall t\leq T_{1}

(E.24)

Consequently, until at least time
T_{1}
, we can use Lemma
E.3
to conclude that
-y_{i_{t}}\ell^{(t)}_{i_{t}}\geq 1-\tau
, which enables lower bounding
A^{(t)}
in the following.

The
i_{t}
-th sample would be used to update parameters, according to our multi-pass SGD updates (
2.5
).
If
i_{t}\in\mathcal{W}
, then
\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t+1)},j\mathbf{u}\rangle=\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},j\mathbf{u}\rangle
holds for any
j\in\{\pm 1\}
and
r\in[m]
.
If
i_{t}\notin\mathcal{W}
but
y_{i_{t}}=-1
, then
\max_{r}\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t+1)},\mathbf{u}\rangle=\max_{r}\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}\rangle\geq 0
since that neuron will not be activated. Otherwise, only if
i_{t}\notin\mathcal{W}
and
y_{i_{t}}=1
, the updating rule becomes

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t+1)},\mathbf{u}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}\rangle+\frac{\eta}{m}\cdot\big(-y_{i_{t}}\ell^{(t)}_{i_{t}}\big)\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}\rangle)\cdot\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}\rangle+\frac{2\eta(1-\tau)\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\max\left\{\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}\rangle,0\right\}.

Take maximum over
r\in[m]
to see that

\displaystyle A^{(t+1)}_{1}\geq A^{(t)}_{1}+\frac{2\eta(1-\tau)\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot A^{(t)}_{1}\geq\exp\left(\frac{\eta(1-\tau)\|\mathbf{u}\|^{2}}{m}\right)\cdot A^{(t)}_{1},

where the last equality is by
1+z\geq\exp(z/2)
for any
0\leq z\leq 2
. Consequently, when
t
is large (larger than
n
), we would have

\displaystyle A^{(t)}_{1}\geq

\displaystyle\exp\left(\frac{\eta(1-\tau)\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\cdot\sum_{t^{\prime}\leq t}\mathbf{1}\{i_{t^{\prime}}\notin\mathcal{W},y_{i_{t}}=1\}\right)\cdot A^{(0)}_{1}

(E.25)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\exp\left(\frac{\eta(1-\tau)\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot(1-\rho)\cdot t}{4m}\right)\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}/2,

(E.26)

at least until step
t\leq T_{1}
.
We use the fact that
\sum_{t^{\prime}\leq t}\mathbf{1}\{i_{t^{\prime}}\notin\mathcal{W},y_{i_{t}}=1\}\geq(1-\rho)t/4
because the sample labels are balanced (Lemma
A.1
) and
1-\rho
proportion of samples come with the strong signal. In the same manner, we would have that

\displaystyle A^{(t)}_{-1}\geq\exp\left(\frac{\eta(1-\tau)\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}(1-\rho)t}{4m}\right)\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}/2.

(E.27)

Define the time when
A^{(t)}_{\pm 1}
both break
\iota
,

\displaystyle T_{2}=\frac{4m}{\eta(1-\tau)(1-\rho)\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{2\iota}{\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}}\right)\leq T_{1},

(E.28)

where the inequality is due to the scaling of
\iota
upon
\tau
.
Moreover, we also need that
T_{2}\leq T_{+}
, where
T_{+}
is the time that
\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}\rangle,\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle
remains in
\mathcal{O}(\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d})
. And this requirement is also achieved by the selection of
\tau
and
\iota
.
Plugging
T_{2}
into the exponential lower bounds (
E.26
) and (
E.27
), we can conclude that

\displaystyle\Phi^{(T_{2})}\geq A^{(T_{2})}_{\pm 1}\geq\iota,

(E.29)

which already grows up to a constant level magnitude by the time
T_{2}
. Lastly, plug the definition of
T_{2}
to upper bound
\psi^{(T_{2})}
as

\displaystyle\Psi^{(T_{2})}\leq\exp\left(\frac{24\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}}{(1-\tau)(1-\rho)\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{2\iota}{\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}}\right)\right)\cdot\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}\leq 2\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}.

(E.30)

In conclusion, by taking
T^{\dagger}=T_{2}
, this lemma is completely proved.
∎

E.2
Stage 2. Exploiting Strong Signal

In the second stage, our lemmas suggest that before the model really learns the weak signal
\mathbf{v}
or memorizes any noise vector, the model already fits a proportion
1-\rho
of the data points (i.e., strong data) by exploiting strong signal
\mathbf{u}
.

Lemma E.6

(Second stage)
.

Under the same conditions as Theorem
E.2
, there exists time

T=T^{\dagger}+\left\lfloor\frac{Cm^{3}}{2\eta\epsilon\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}\right\rfloor

such that: (i) the average loss over iterations within this stage has decreased to
2\epsilon
, i.e.,

\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{W}^{c}}\min_{T^{\dagger}\leq t\leq T}\left\{y_{i}-f(\bm{x}_{i};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\right\}^{2}\leq 3\epsilon,

(ii) all through the training dynamics
0\leq t\leq T
, there holds that

\displaystyle\max_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle\right|\leq 2\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\sigma_{0}\cdot\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2},

(E.31)

(iii) all through the training dynamics
0\leq t\leq T
, there holds that

\displaystyle\max_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m],i\in[n]}\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\right|\leq\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d},\quad\max_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m],i\in\mathcal{W}}\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|\leq\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d}.

(E.32)

In studying the second stage, we firstly identify when the upper bound on
\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle
breaks and find that the conclusions of Lemma
E.5
still holds before that time.

Lemma E.7
.

Under the same conditions as Theorem
E.2
, take
\eta\leq m/6\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}
. There exists a time

\displaystyle T^{\ddagger}=\frac{m}{6\eta\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{\sqrt{\tau/2}}{\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}}\right)\geq T^{\dagger}

(E.33)

such that (
E.12
) and

\displaystyle\max_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle\right|\leq 2\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{v}\|,\quad\max_{r\in[m]}\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},j\mathbf{u}\rangle\geq\iota/2,\quad\forall j\in\{\pm 1\},

(E.34)

hold for any
T^{\dagger}\leq t\leq T^{\ddagger}
.

Proof of Lemma
E.7
.

Firstly, we need to adopt the exponential upper bound derived in proving Lemma
E.5
,

\Psi^{(t)}\leq\exp\left(\frac{6\eta\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}t}{m}\right)\cdot\Psi^{(0)}\leq\exp\left(\frac{6\eta\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}t}{m}\right)\cdot\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}.

Then we naturally find that before
T^{\ddagger}
, it would always hold that

\displaystyle\max_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\left|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}\rangle\right|\leq 2\sqrt{2\log(16m/p)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}.

(E.35)

Due to the conditions on
\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}/\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}
,
T^{\ddagger}
is found to be much larger than
T^{\dagger}
.
Then we proceed to prove the other assertion by induction.
At time
t=T^{\dagger}
, the lower bound
\max_{j,r}\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},j\mathbf{u}\rangle\geq\iota/2
holds as a consequence of the previous lemma.
Suppose it holds until time
t
.
If
i_{t}\in\mathcal{W}
, then
\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t+1)},j\mathbf{u}\rangle=\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},j\mathbf{u}\rangle
holds for any
j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]
.
If
i_{t}\notin\mathcal{W}
but
y_{i_{t}}=-1
, then
\max_{r\in[m]}\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t+1)},\mathbf{u}\rangle=\max_{r\in[m]}\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}\rangle\geq 0
since that neuron will not be activated.
Otherwise, if
i_{t}\notin\mathcal{W}
and
y_{i_{t}}=1
, consider the updating rule

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t+1)},\mathbf{u}\rangle=\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}\rangle+\frac{\eta}{m}\cdot\big(1-y_{i_{t}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\big)\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}\rangle)\cdot\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2},

(E.36)

from which we find
\max_{r\in[m],i_{t}\notin\mathcal{W}}\langle\mathbf{w}_{y_{i},r}^{(t+1)},y_{i}\mathbf{u}\rangle\geq\max_{r\in[m],i_{t}\notin\mathcal{W}}\langle\mathbf{w}_{y_{i},r}^{(t)},y_{i}\mathbf{u}\rangle
must hold if
y_{i_{t}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\leq 1
. Otherwise, once
y_{i_{t}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})>1
, it immediately follows that

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle<y_{i_{t}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})=F_{y_{i_{t}}}(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})-F_{-{y_{i_{t}}}}(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})

(E.37)

\displaystyle\leq F_{y_{i_{t}}}(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r\in[m]}\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},y_{i_{t}}\mathbf{u}\rangle)+\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},y_{i_{t}}\mathbf{v}\rangle)+\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{\xi}_{i_{t}}\rangle)

(E.38)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{r\in[m]}\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}\rangle^{2}+\sigma_{0}^{2}\|\mathbf{v}\|^{2}+\sigma_{0}^{2}\sigma_{p}^{2}d.
