(E.39)

Consequently, for the specific neuron
r^{\ast}=\mathop{\mathrm{argmax}}_{r\in[m]}\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}\rangle^{2}
, there holds

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r^{\ast}}^{(t+1)},\mathbf{u}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r^{\ast}}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}\rangle-\frac{3\eta}{m}\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r^{\ast}}^{(t)},\mathbf{u}\rangle\cdot\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}

(E.40)

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-8\log(16m/p)\cdot\sigma_{0}^{2}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}-\sigma_{0}^{2}\sigma_{p}^{2}d\right)\cdot\left(1-\frac{3\eta}{m}\cdot\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}\right)

(E.41)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\iota}{2},

(E.42)

where the last inequality is enabled by taking

\displaystyle\eta\leq\frac{m}{6\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}},\quad\sigma_{0}\leq\sqrt{\frac{1-\iota}{8\log(16m/p)\cdot\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}+\sigma_{p}^{2}d}}.

(E.43)

Therefore, we find that
\max_{r\in[m]}\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(t+1)},\mathbf{u}\rangle\geq\iota/2
must hold no matter what
i_{t}
is. In the same way, one can also obtain
\max_{r\in[m]}\langle\mathbf{w}_{-1,r}^{(t+1)},-\mathbf{u}\rangle\geq\iota/2
.
By induction, the induction proof is complete.
∎

Our subsequently analysis confirms that even before
T^{\ddagger}
, the model can already fit those data points with strong signal by exploiting
\mathbf{u}
.
For the given
0<\epsilon<1
, define a reference point
\mathbf{W}^{\ast}
as

\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{\ast}=\frac{4m(1+\epsilon)}{\iota}\cdot\frac{j\mathbf{u}}{\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}},\quad j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m].

(E.44)

Lemma E.8
.

Under the same condition as the previous lemma, for all
T^{\dagger}\leq t\leq T^{\ddagger}
, there holds

\displaystyle y_{i}\langle\nabla f(\mathbf{x}_{i};\mathbf{W}^{(t)}),\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\rangle\geq 2\cdot(1+\epsilon)

(E.45)

for any
i\notin\mathcal{W}
.

Proof of Lemma
E.8
.

Recall that the definition of CNN in (
2.3
)
and that
\mathbf{u}\perp\mathrm{span}(\mathbf{v},\bm{\xi}_{i})
.
We have that

\displaystyle y_{i}\langle\nabla f(\mathbf{x}_{i};\mathbf{W}^{(t)}),\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y_{i}\mathbf{u}\rangle)\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{\ast},y_{i}\mathbf{u}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y_{i}\mathbf{u}\rangle)\cdot\frac{4(1+\epsilon)}{\iota}

\displaystyle\geq\max_{r\in[m]}\langle\mathbf{w}_{y_{i},r}^{(t)},y_{i}\mathbf{u}\rangle\cdot\frac{4(1+\epsilon)}{\iota}

\displaystyle\geq 2\cdot(1+\epsilon),

where the last inequality is by
\max_{r}\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},j\mathbf{u}\rangle\geq\iota/2
for any
j\in\{\pm 1\}
as shown by the previous lemma.
∎

Lemma E.9
.

Continued from the previous setting, for
T^{\dagger}\leq t\leq T^{\ddagger}
, if
i_{t}\notin\mathcal{W}
, there holds

\|\mathbf{W}^{(t)}-\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\|_{F}^{2}-\|\mathbf{W}^{(t+1)}-\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\|_{F}^{2}\geq 2\eta\big(f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})-y_{i_{t}}\big)^{2}-2\eta\epsilon^{2}.

Proof of Lemma
E.9
.

Firstly we expand the difference by

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{W}^{(t)}-\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\|_{F}^{2}-\|\mathbf{W}^{(t+1)}-\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad=2\eta\langle\ell^{(t)}_{i_{t}}\nabla f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)}),\mathbf{W}^{(t)}-\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\rangle-\eta^{2}\cdot\left|\ell^{(t)}_{i_{t}}\right|^{2}\cdot\|\nabla f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\|_{F}^{2}.

(E.46)

Since the neural network
f(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W})
is
2
-homogeneous in
\mathbf{W}
due to the activation function
\sigma(z)=\max\{z,0\}^{2}
, we have that

\langle\nabla f(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)}),\mathbf{W}^{(t)}\rangle=2f(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{W}^{(t)}).

Stack these observations into the first term of previous difference expansion to obtain

\displaystyle\langle\ell^{(t)}_{i_{t}}\nabla f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)}),\mathbf{W}^{(t)}-\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\rangle

\displaystyle=\ell^{(t)}_{i_{t}}\cdot\big(2f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})-\langle\nabla f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)}),\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\rangle\big)

\displaystyle=2\ell^{(t)}_{i_{t}}\cdot\big(f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})-y_{i_{t}}\big)+\ell^{(t)}_{i_{t}}\cdot y_{i_{t}}\cdot\big(2-y_{i_{t}}\langle\nabla f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)}),\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\rangle\big).

Note that the first term is exactly
2(f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})-y_{i_{t}})^{2}
. As for the second term, since
i_{t}\notin\mathcal{W}
, we need to plug in Lemma
E.8
to see
2-y_{i_{t}}\langle\nabla f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)}),\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\rangle\leq-2\epsilon<0
, so that

\displaystyle\left|\ell^{(t)}_{i_{t}}\cdot y_{i_{t}}\cdot\big(2-y_{i_{t}}\langle\nabla f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)}),\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\rangle\big)\right|\leq\frac{1}{2}\big(\ell^{(t)}_{i_{t}}\big)^{2}+2\epsilon^{2}.

As a result, we would know that

\displaystyle\langle\ell^{(t)}_{i_{t}}\nabla f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)}),\mathbf{W}^{(t)}-\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\rangle\geq\frac{3}{2}(f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})-y_{i_{t}})^{2}-2\epsilon^{2}.

(E.47)

Next, an upper bound on the second order term
\eta^{2}\|\nabla L(\mathbf{W}^{(t})\|_{F}^{2}
is given by

\displaystyle\eta^{2}\cdot\left|\ell^{(t)}_{i_{t}}\right|^{2}\cdot\|\nabla f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad=\eta^{2}\cdot\ell^{(t)2}_{i_{t}}\cdot\left(\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\sum_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y\mathbf{u}\rangle)^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\left.+\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\sum_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y\mathbf{v}\rangle)^{2}+\|\bm{\xi}_{i_{t}}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\sum_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i_{t}}\rangle)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\mathcal{O}(\max\{\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2},\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2},\|\bm{\xi}_{i_{t}}\|_{2}^{2}\})\cdot\eta^{2}\cdot\ell^{(t)2}_{i_{t}},

since the dynamics of inner products
\langle\mathbf{w}^{(t)}_{j,r},y\mathbf{u}\rangle,\langle\mathbf{w}^{(t)}_{j,r},y\mathbf{v}\rangle,\langle\mathbf{w}^{(t)}_{j,r},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle
are well bounded by
\mathcal{O}(1)
. Via scaling
\eta\cdot\mathcal{O}(\max\{\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2},\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2},\|\bm{\xi}_{i_{t}}\|_{2}^{2}\})\leq 1
, we would know
\eta^{2}|\ell^{(t)}_{i_{t}}|^{2}\|\nabla f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\|_{F}^{2}\leq\eta\ell^{(t)2}_{i_{t}}
. Eventually, continued from (
E.46
), we can completely prove this lemma.
∎

Lemma E.10
.

Continued from the previous setting, for
T^{\dagger}\leq t\leq T^{\ddagger}
, if
i_{t}\in\mathcal{W}
, there holds

\|\mathbf{W}^{(t)}-\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\|_{F}^{2}-\|\mathbf{W}^{(t+1)}-\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\|_{F}^{2}\geq-C\eta\sigma_{0}^{2}\cdot\big(\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}+\sigma_{p}^{2}d\big).

(E.48)

Proof of Lemma
E.10
.

Same as the last lemma, from the SGD setting, we have that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{W}^{(t)}-\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\|_{F}^{2}-\|\mathbf{W}^{(t+1)}-\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad=2\eta\langle\ell^{(t)}_{i_{t}}\nabla f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)}),\mathbf{W}^{(t)}-\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\rangle-\eta^{2}\cdot\left|\ell^{(t)}_{i_{t}}\right|^{2}\cdot\|\nabla f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\|_{F}^{2},

(E.49)

and from the
2
-homogeneity, it follows that

\langle\nabla f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)}),\mathbf{W}^{(t)}\rangle=2f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)}).

Since
i_{t}\in\mathcal{W}
, every
\nabla_{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}}f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})
is in
\mathrm{span}(\mathbf{v},\bm{\xi}_{i_{t}},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i_{t}})\perp\mathbf{u}
, so

\langle\nabla f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)}),\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\rangle=0.

As a result, the first term in (
E.49
) can be bounded by

\displaystyle\left|2\eta\langle\ell^{(t)}_{i_{t}}\nabla f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)}),\mathbf{W}^{(t)}-\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\qquad=4\eta\cdot\left|\big(f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})-y_{i_{t}}\big)\cdot f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{12\eta}{m}\cdot\left|\sum_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i_{t}}\rangle)+\sum_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y_{i_{t}}\mathbf{v}\rangle)+\sum_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i_{t}}\rangle)\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\mathcal{O}\big(\eta\sigma_{0}^{2}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}+\eta\sigma_{0}^{2}\sigma_{p}^{2}d\big).

We can also deal with the second term in (
E.49
) by

\displaystyle\eta^{2}\cdot\left|\ell^{(t)}_{i_{t}}\right|^{2}\cdot\|\nabla f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\eta^{2}\cdot\ell^{(t)2}_{i_{t}}\cdot\left(\|\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i_{t}}\|^{2}_{2}\cdot\sum_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i_{t}}\rangle)^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\left.+\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\sum_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y_{i_{t}}\mathbf{v}\rangle)^{2}+\|\bm{\xi}_{i_{t}}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\sum_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i_{t}}\rangle)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\mathcal{O}\big(\eta^{2}(\sigma_{0}^{4}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{4}+\sigma_{0}^{4}\sigma_{p}^{4}d^{2})\big),

where the last inequality is due to
\ell^{(t)}_{i_{t}}
being
\mathcal{O}(1)
. Since we already take
\eta\leq m/6\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}
, the second term of (
E.49
) is ignorable compared to the first term of (
E.49
). Therefore, we can conclude (
E.48
).
∎

Equipped with Lemmas
E.7
,
E.8
,
E.9
, and
E.10
, we are ready to prove the main lemma of second stage.

Proof of Lemma
E.6
.

Continued from Lemmas
E.9
and
E.10
, for any
t\geq T^{\dagger}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{t-T^{\dagger}+1}\sum_{s=T^{\dagger}}^{t}\mathbf{1}\{i_{s}\notin\mathcal{W}\}\cdot\frac{1}{2}\big(f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})-y_{i_{t}}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{\|\mathbf{W}^{(T^{\dagger})}-\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\|_{F}^{2}}{2\eta(t-T^{\dagger}+1)}+\epsilon^{2}(1-\rho)+C\sigma_{0}^{2}\big(\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}+\sigma_{p}^{2}d\big)\rho.

Before proceeding to scale time
t
, it is helpful to decompose
\|\mathbf{W}^{(T^{\dagger})}-\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\|_{F}^{2}
and have an upper bound,

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{W}^{(T^{\dagger})}-\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(T^{\dagger})}-\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{\ast},\mathbf{u}\rangle^{2}}{\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}+\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(T^{\dagger})}-\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{\ast},\mathbf{v}\rangle^{2}}{\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}}+\left\|\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\xi},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}}(\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(T^{\dagger})}-\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{\ast})\right\|^{2}_{2}

(E.50)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\|\left(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\frac{\mathbf{v}\mathbf{v}^{\top}}{\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}}-\frac{\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{\top}}{\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}-\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\xi},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}}\right)(\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(T^{\dagger})}-\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{\ast})\right\|^{2}_{2}

(E.51)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\frac{2\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(T^{\dagger})},\mathbf{u}\rangle^{2}+2\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{\ast},\mathbf{u}\rangle^{2}}{\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}+\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(T^{\dagger})},\mathbf{v}\rangle^{2}}{\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}}+\left\|\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\xi},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}}\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(T^{\dagger})}\right\|^{2}

(E.52)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\|\left(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\frac{\mathbf{v}\mathbf{v}^{\top}}{\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}}-\frac{\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{\top}}{\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}}-\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\xi},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}}\right)(\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(T^{\dagger})}-\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{\ast})\right\|_{2}^{2},

(E.53)

where
\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\xi},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}}
denotes the projection matrix onto linear space
\mathrm{span}_{i\in[n],i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}}(\bm{\xi}_{i},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i^{\prime}})
.
In these derivations, we exploit the fact that
\mathbf{w}^{\ast}
is parallel to
\mathbf{u}
, and the gradient steps only updates
\mathbf{w}
along the directions of
\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}
.
Recall that by Lemma
E.7
,

\displaystyle\max_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(T^{\dagger})},j\mathbf{u}\rangle=\Omega(1),\quad\max_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\left|\langle\mathbf{w}^{(T^{\dagger})}_{j,r},\mathbf{v}\rangle\right|=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}),\quad\|\mathbf{w}^{(0)}_{j,r}\|_{2}=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\sigma_{0}\sqrt{d}),

(E.54)

\displaystyle\max_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m],i\in[n]}\left|\langle\mathbf{w}^{(T^{\dagger})}_{j,r},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\right|=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d}),

(E.55)

the leading term in (
E.53
) is
\sum_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{\ast},\mathbf{u}\rangle^{2}/\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}
.
Therefore, we would conclude that
\|\mathbf{W}^{(T^{\dagger})}-\mathbf{W}^{\ast}\|_{F}^{2}\leq Cm^{3}/\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}
. As a result, the average loss after iterations
T^{\dagger}
can be bounded by

\displaystyle\frac{1}{t-T^{\dagger}+1}\sum_{s=T^{\dagger}}^{t}\mathbf{1}\{i_{s}\notin\mathcal{W}\}\cdot\frac{1}{2}\big(f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})-y_{i_{t}}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{Cm^{3}}{2\eta\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}(t-T^{\dagger}+1)}+\epsilon^{2}(1-\rho)+C\sigma_{0}^{2}(\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}+\sigma_{p}^{2}d)\rho.

Then choose
T=T^{\dagger}+\lfloor Cm^{3}/(2\eta\epsilon\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2})\rfloor
as stated in Lemma
E.6
.
Since
\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2}/\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}\geq\widetilde{\Omega}(m^{2})
, we can verify that
T\leq T^{\ddagger}
where
T^{\ddagger}
is given in Lemma
E.7
until when the weak signal cannot be fully learned. Moreover, we also have
T\leq T_{+}
where
T_{+}
is given in Lemma
E.4
when the noise is not memorized.

In conclusion, via scaling
\sigma_{0}^{2}\leq\epsilon/(C\rho(\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}+\sigma_{p}^{2}d))
, the final output would be

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{W}^{c}}\min_{T^{\dagger}\leq t\leq T}\left\{y_{i}-f(\mathbf{x}_{i};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{t-T^{\dagger}+1}\sum_{s=T^{\dagger}}^{t}\mathbf{1}\{i_{s}\notin\mathcal{W}\}\cdot\frac{1}{2}\big(f(\mathbf{x}_{i_{t}};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})-y_{i_{t}}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon+\epsilon^{2}(1-\rho)+C\sigma_{0}^{2}\big(\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}+\sigma_{p}^{2}d\big)\rho

\displaystyle\leq 3\epsilon,

ending the proof of Lemma
E.6
.
∎

E.3
Stage 3. Memorizing Noise

After the second stage, the model already fits those data points with strong signal by exploiting
\mathbf{u}
. Subsequently, in the following third stage, the residual
\ell^{(t)}_{i}
for
i\in\mathcal{W}^{c}
would remain quite small, preventing the model from learning
\mathbf{u}
.

On the contrary, since
f(\mathbf{x}_{i};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\sigma_{0}^{2})
is still far from its label
y_{i}
for each sample
i\in\mathcal{W}
without the strong signal
\mathbf{u}
.
Therefore, the weight vectors would still evolve in the directions perpendicular to
\mathbf{u}
. In Assumption
E.1
, the ratio between the weak signal
\mathbf{v}
and the typical noise norm
\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d}
is scaled by

\displaystyle\frac{\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}}{\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d}}\leq\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right).

(E.56)

Therefore, the model will eventually interpolates the whole dataset by memorizing noise vectors
(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i},\bm{\xi}_{i}),i\in\mathcal{W}
, Now we define a new reference point
\mathbf{W}^{\star}
by

\mathbf{w}^{\star}_{j,r}=\mathbf{w}^{\ast}_{j,r}+\frac{4m(1+\epsilon^{\prime})}{\iota}\cdot\left(\sum_{i\in\mathcal{W}}\mathbf{1}\{y_{i}=j\}\cdot\frac{\bm{\xi}_{i}}{\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}}+\mathbf{1}\{y_{i}=j\}\cdot\frac{\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}}{\|\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}\|_{2}}\right),\quad j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m],

where
\mathbf{w}^{\ast}_{j,r}
defined in (
E.44
) is the reference point we used in the second stage. The following lemma is an adaptation of Theorem 4.4 of
Cao et al. 2022
onto SGD with square loss.

Lemma E.11

(Third stage)
.

Under the same conditions as Theorem
E.2
, for some
\epsilon^{\prime}\in(0,1)
, let

T^{\prime}=T+\left\lfloor\frac{\left\|\mathbf{W}^{(T)}-\mathbf{W}^{\star}\right\|_{F}^{2}}{2\eta\epsilon}\right\rfloor,

where
T
is the end of the second stage in Lemma
E.6
.
Then we would have that

\displaystyle\max_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}\left|\langle\mathbf{w}^{(t)}_{j,r},\mathbf{v}\rangle\right|\leq\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}),

(E.57)

even until
t\leq T^{\prime}
.
But the whole dataset has already been interpolated during this interval,

\frac{1}{2\rho n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{W}}\min_{T\leq t\leq T^{\prime}}\left\{y_{i}-f(\mathbf{x}_{i};\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\right\}^{2}\leq 3\epsilon^{\prime}.

Proof of Lemma
E.11
.

As the closing stage of the training dynamics, the evolution dynamics during this interval is straightforward based on all techniques developed in Appendices
C
and
E
. Inner products

\displaystyle\left\{\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle,\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right\}_{i\in\mathcal{W}}

(E.58)

would firstly go through a substage in which they exponentially increase to a constant level (just as stage 1). And then the model will fit all samples indexed by
\mathcal{W}
by memorizing these noise vectors in polynomial time. All through this interval,
\max_{j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m]}|\langle\mathbf{w}^{(t)}_{j,r},\mathbf{v}\rangle|
would stay
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\sigma_{0}\|\mathbf{v}\|)
due to the scale of
\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}/(\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d})
in (
E.56
).
A detailed proof is omitted here for readability.
∎

Combine Lemmas
E.5
,
E.6
and
E.11
to obtain the full version of Theorem
E.2
.
